#+TITLE: Scope-aware runtime import pruning
#+SETUPFILE: ../modeling/org/setup.org
#+CATEGORY: refactoring

* RESEARCH Runtime import pruning
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: runtime-import-pruning
:OWNER: refactoring
:STATUS: prototype
:EVIDENCE_CLASS: source-correspondence-and-local-differential-tests
:END:

This work preserves ordinary Gerbil imports, including =(import :std/io)=, and
uses resolved binding demand to reduce native loading. The [[https://git.cons.io/mighty-gerbils/gerbil/pulls/1430][Gerbil staging integration]]
is the integration reference. [[https://github.com/tao3k/gerbil/issues/2][Gerbil issue 2]] tracks the import closure investigation and earlier consumer
qualification. The local experiments used Gerbil
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= and its pinned Gambit
=b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228=, with experimental compiler and
loader changes. The DEV record tracks the Gerbil/Gambit boundary and the
conditions for admitting those changes.

** Engineering contract

- Resolve actual references after expansion, including references introduced by
  transformers and references through reexports. Preserve the defining binding,
  phase, and module instance across expansion, compilation, and loading.
- Use required initialization and its binding references as roots alongside
  demanded code. Preserve observable effects in the sequence of the ordinary
  entry point. Name lookup may leave a transformer dormant when obtaining the
  interface requires no transformer evaluation.
- Keep a single owner for activation in the existing loader and module
  registry. The evaluator may know an interface while some implementations
  remain deferred. A later explicit import or binding read must find the
  original instance, activate it at most once, and let subsequent reads use it.
- Admit the existing in-memory native body only when its recorded load identity
  matches the artifact and the dependency proof. Missing or obsolete proof and
  opaque native or interpreted effects require the ordinary path at that
  boundary. Keep that fallback active through the affected import traversal.
- Treat a mixed object as an ordinary eager unit when its effects cannot be
  separated safely. Use existing module metadata and loader entry points to
  avoid a parallel registry, hand-maintained edge tables, and extra native
  objects.

The lexical question is which binding a reference denotes. Manual narrowing
changes which bindings are available and may also change which modules execute.
Automatic pruning keeps that interface and must preserve what the unmodified
program can observe, including effects reached without a lexically named
export. Scope determines the dependency edges that can be proven, while the
loader controls when their implementations become available.

** Research and source correspondence

| Research | Engineering consequence |
|----------+-------------------------|
| [[https://legacy.cs.indiana.edu/~dyb/pubs/popl99.pdf][Waddell and Dybvig, Extending the Scope of Syntactic Abstraction, POPL 1999, section 5]] | Observe the expanded binding identity. An exported transformer can expose an otherwise private binding to a separately compiled consumer. |
| [[https://xivilization.net/~marek/tex/hellprog/papers/implicit-phasing.pdf][Ghuloum and Dybvig, Implicit Phasing for R6RS Libraries, ICFP 2007, sections 4.3 and 5]] | Separate library information, transformer visitation, and variable initialization. Use actual references to choose the phase requirements and follow reexports to their owners. |
| [[https://home.dericilab.live/assets/docs/papers/icfp19-rrocs.pdf][Flatt et al., Rebuilding Racket on Chez Scheme, ICFP 2019, section 3]] | Expose imports and exports by evaluation phase to the compiler. Retain mutable binding locations when sharing an instantiated module. |
| [[https://suif.stanford.edu/~jwhaley/papers/aplas05r.pdf][Livshits, Whaley, and Lam, Reflection Analysis for Java, APLAS 2005]] | Prove targets when possible and identify sites where input-dependent reflection requires a conservative answer. |

The vendored Chez revision =41d5ffca7a07cbfc8807e719dedcc70ed74811cd= has
separate compile-time and run-time descriptors with distinct ordered
requirements. =visit-loaded-library= and =invoke-loaded-library= handle pending
operations and restore the operation on failure. The compiler emits separate
compile-time and run-time library installation forms. Gerbil can reuse the
separation of responsibilities while keeping its own module instantiation and
effect order. The [[https://cisco.github.io/ChezScheme/csug9.5/system.html][Chez whole-program compilation documentation]] also calls out
initialization-time =eval= as a source of dynamic dependencies.
[[file:../../vendor/chez/s/syntax.ss::2377][Chez descriptors and activation]]
[[file:../../vendor/chez/s/compile.ss::710][Chez compiler emission]]

** Lean demand and cost model

[[file:../../models/build/RuntimeImportDemand.lean][RuntimeImportDemand.lean]]
implements a bounded name resolver and a worklist planner. The name
resolver follows requested aliases to the original owner, phase, and
slot. A name lookup, macro execution, runtime value reference, library
visit, required initialization, and physical image are distinct requests.
The worklist reads dependency rows only when a request reaches them.

An accepted plan is the least closed set containing the expanded binding
references and required initialization roots. Lean proves successful
search exact, sufficient fuel complete for a finite domain with complete
rows, and changes to unread rows irrelevant to the result. An exact
and admissible manual set agrees with the automatic set. Missing or
stale required rows and opaque observations choose ordinary evaluation.
Observable initialization remains in its original order by filtering the
reference schedule. A separate weighted objective accounts for discovery,
checking, and execution. Fewer images imply a startup benefit only when
the avoided work exceeds added discovery and checking.

The design uses the split between metadata, visit, and invocation in
[[https://xivilization.net/~marek/tex/hellprog/papers/implicit-phasing.pdf][Implicit Phasing for R6RS Libraries, sections 4 and 5]].
The vendored Chez entry points follow separate ordered visit and invoke
requirements. The distinction between resolved variables and source
names is also explicit in
[[https://home.dericilab.live/assets/docs/papers/icfp19-rrocs.pdf][Rebuilding Racket on Chez Scheme, section 3]].
Gerbil can keep its existing module registry and emit stable binding,
phase, initialization, and image identities at compilation. The runtime
then opens only metadata reachable from the actual expanded scope.
This requires a name lookup path that need not first expand the full
reexport chain and its unrelated imports.

The source review found that =core-import-module= calls
=core-expand-module-begin= before publishing the compiled interface.
The stock import expander resolves a library path through
=import-module= while expanding that interface. Cutting image loads
after that traversal can preserve a substantial part of the startup
cost. The four diagnostic runs on this checkpoint produced identical
first-receipt image sets for each arm with and without a separate
pre-expansion call. The automatic arm retained the original
=TMPDIR= observation. The manual arm used the changed value. The
diagnostic timing campaign was stopped at the reboot checkpoint and
its 17 raw observations have no aggregate performance conclusion.

The Lean kernel was checked with the pinned Lean 4.34.1 toolchain and
warnings as errors. Its 17 executable checks include a renamed
reexport, a phase-helper cycle, observable initialization omitted by a
manual cut, and opaque or stale input. The kernel assumes exact
compiler-derived dependency rows and an authoritative effect schedule.
Gerbil integration, native performance parity, and the full package
gates remain open.

The original Codex worktree disappeared after reboot. Its checkpoint, experimental
source diff, and 17 partial timing samples are unavailable. The Lean source and the
historical observations recorded here survived.

** 2026-11-19 resumed name lookup experiment

The staging PR 1430 head was rechecked at
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=, with Gambit gitlink
=b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228=. The executable differential experiment
used the installed Homebrew SDK (Gerbil =1cfb032= on Gambit =ea114fc=).
The surviving Lean model typechecked again with Lean 4.34.1 and warnings as errors.

The generator uses Gerbil's =core-resolve-module-export= and =resolve-identifier=
on the actual =(import :std/io)=. All 986 distinct (public name, phase) keys from
1,126 export entries agreed with ordinary lookup after resolving imports. There
were 690 value bindings and 296 syntax bindings. The lookup reader fetched at
most ten rows per key, preserved the module registry across all comparisons,
and fell back on a mismatched generation, a missing phase, or a truncated
directory.

Twenty fresh-process pairs alternated which arm ran first. The filesystem was
warm. The selected receipts cover =tcp-connect=, =call-with-temporary-file=,
=with-buffered-reader=, and =BufferedReader=, and matched for all trials.

| Lookup arm | Median launch to exit | Observed range | Additional module contexts |
|------------+-----------------------+----------------+----------------------------|
| Ordinary expander | 1,305.458 ms | 795.651–1,702.266 ms | 94 |
| Prototype, precomputed metadata | 287.264 ms | 225.303–523.570 ms | 0 |

These measurements cover export-name lookup. The comparison neither invokes the
resolved procedures nor executes the selected transformers. The attempted dyld
trace emitted no records. The observed counts measure compiler module
registrations. Runtime initialization, physical image reduction, macro execution,
dynamic eval, and consumer performance require their own comparison.

A renamed reexport fixture tests the boundary between asking for a macro
binding and executing it. The index returned the provider's syntax identity
before module activation with the registry unchanged. Both arms then ran the
ordinary activation path. They produced the same trace: provider initialization
followed by façade initialization, one module instance on repeated import, then
the phase-one helper on dynamic eval. The macro generated a private runtime
reference and the final value receipt was =(73 9)=. The expanded
=%#ref= independently resolved to =provider#private-runtime= at phase zero. This control separates
name lookup from actual activation; the production activation path remains
the reference for the integration test.

A strict module context rejects two distinct strong imports with the same
consumer name and phase even if the name has no references. The
=unused-conflict= fixture reproduced this on the installed SDK. The same
source, entered as a top-level script, printed =42= under that context's
rebinding rules. Lean now proves that demand-projected visibility checks
see only collisions within the requested names. Preserve ordinary
visibility checks, or reuse their result when the selected metadata and
import environment match, before using demand to defer activation.

For integration, =compile-meta-code= owns interface emission and
=core-import-module= owns loading and publication. Index lookup before
=core-expand-module-begin= can avoid building interface contexts just to discover
a binding. The existing evaluator will still need the original syntax binding
and module context when that binding is used. All 296 syntax keys in this
=std/io= interface resolve to =user-expander= objects on the installed
SDK. Following the import wrappers reaches the same context identifier
as the transformer's stored lexical context for all 296. This gives
the producer a source for the owner field without parsing binding names.
Other expander kinds still need their own correspondence or fallback. Real admission needs to bind saved
metadata to the selected artifact and existing load path. The prototype
generation label is a test input. The flat prototype index is
108,713 bytes for the 120-byte installed =std/io.ssi= façade. The experiment
does not establish a storage format for widespread use. Measure production
metadata size and avoid duplicating complete transitive interfaces across
reexporters.

** 2026-11-19 expanded demand and native loader control

A closed package compiled with the Homebrew SDK (=1cfb032= on =ea114fc=)
uses an ordinary import through a renamed reexport. The compiler prototype
extends the existing expression visitor and records defining module, phase,
and binding ID. Source-level spelling does not determine ownership. A
separate scan admits literal and procedure definitions as dormant and
collects eager initializer and entry roots. It walks the actual references
from those roots. The generated loader is checked against the stock
=apply-generate-loader= topology. The selected phase-zero bodies load
through the existing registry in original dependency order.

Only the consumer's generated loader and its compiled object differ.
The public interfaces and dependency loaders are identical. A transitive
branch with no reachable binding or eager initializer is absent through
the first receipt. Initializer B observed initializer A's earlier write
and both arms returned =(42 a)=. A later dynamic =import= and =eval= through
the original façade loaded the omitted branch and returned =99=. Resetting
shared state before this import confirmed that the first two initializers did
not execute again.

| 20 alternating process pairs | Ordinary | Candidate |
|------------------------------+----------+-----------|
| Fixture native images at first receipt | 15 | 6 |
| Launch to first receipt, median | 135.996 ms | 128.911 ms |
| Launch to natural exit, median | 292.291 ms | 281.823 ms |
| Peak RSS, median | 93.61 MiB | 93.22 MiB |

The paired median savings to first receipt and exit were 7.111 and
12.732 ms. Both ranges include trials favoring ordinary. Peak RSS fell in
all 20 pairs. This fixture exercises a closed scope with ordinary loader
paths. It supplies no aggregate performance claim for standard-library,
MCP, or POO workloads.

An eager alias to the omitted implementation retained both its provider
and the provider's dependency. A deliberately incorrect loader for that
alias still printed its initial success marker after the native load warned
about an undefined variable. Observing and calling the alias then failed
with an unbound variable. The ordinary arm returned =99=. Missing source
proof and opaque dynamic eval selected the ordinary path.

The staging =std/io/socket/datagram= body expands to six procedure
definitions. The exact staging =socket/api= source, compiled to Scheme
on this SDK, eagerly reads three of those procedures and then registers
them with =bind-method!= for =DatagramSocket=. An importer that keeps the
unmodified =socket/api= initializer must keep that dependency. Resuming
the earlier method-initializer work requires preserving MOP lookup and
registration order, including the original method value on observation.
The scope mechanism cannot classify a procedure alias as inert merely
because the provider's definitions are procedures.

** 2026-11-19 manual startup parity and real socket scope

The checked Lean model now separates =prepareStartup= from
=activateStartup=. Ordinary expansion establishes the roots and import
visibility. Preparation computes the exact closure and ordered native
action sequence. Activation has no dependency or export table input.
It selects the prepared sequence only for matching consumer, interface,
and native image selections. An opaque observation or mismatched selection
returns control to ordinary evaluation before planned actions begin.
The model assumes the loader supplies authenticated selection identity.

=activate_matches_manual_schedule= proves equality of the ordered action
sequences when a hand-selected scope is admissible and minimal under the
same observable roots. =startup_manual_cost_parity= proves, within the
additive work model, that equal action sequences reach parity exactly
when automatic discovery, validation and dispatch cost no more than the
same work in the manual path. =startup_manual_cost_gate= gives the
conservative form using an upper bound for automatic overhead and a
lower bound for manual overhead. The model assumes no performance
tolerance. Native comparison needs matching inputs, phase and initialization
receipts, and physical actions. The runtime cost of establishing identity
and dispatching the sequence belongs in the timing. The full benchmark
must still measure first receipt, exit, and peak RSS.

The manual comparator is now anchored to the exact five-file diff
published as =6a08754= in Gerbil Issue 2. It applies cleanly to the
current staging source and passes the Gerbil MCP balance checks and
module expansion. It changes the JSON reader, writer, I/O and utilities,
and the LL1 parser. Keep that comparator for performance. Preserve the
ordinary program's earlier =TMPDIR= snapshot when evaluating automatic
pruning, since the hand-narrowed graph captures a different value.
The historical consumer comparison records 57 fewer loading entries
from this same patch, well above the three-image gain in the socket
control below. Its published time numbers describe the previous SDK
and need a matched current rerun.

The physical control uses a single compiled TCP consumer with ordinary
=std/io= imports. The experimental loader derives the three datagram
procedure aliases and the class's prototype from the emitted Scheme
body. The three arms use the same consumer code. The eager and deferred
variants share the exact same candidate objects. The deferred scope
returns the same TCP byte and temporarily omits the API wrapper, datagram
wrapper, and datagram body. Later ordinary import recovers all three
procedure identities. Twelve concurrent requests return the same wrapper,
and both original and deferred arms pass all six original socket cases
in the same processes after the TCP receipt.

| Twenty rounds | Original | Same-candidate eager | Deferred |
|---------------+----------+----------------------+----------|
| Native objects before the TCP receipt | 192 | 192 | 189 |
| First receipt, median | 458.656 ms | 455.795 ms | 443.269 ms |
| Process wall time, median | 2299.132 ms | 2241.164 ms | 2205.954 ms |
| Peak RSS, median | 250.35 MiB | 250.41 MiB | 250.84 MiB |

Deferred first receipt was faster in twelve pairs against either comparator.
The matched paired median saving was 9.371 ms, with regressions in other
pairs. The matched paired median at process exit favored eager by
7.541 ms. The observed peak RSS increased. These runs cover a selected
socket scope. Manual performance parity and the consumer benchmarks
remain open.

Two observations constrain admission. The public parent-class properties
can expose the datagram subclass and thus its method table. The model
connects that observation to the ordered binding and prototype actions,
even when the consumer has no lexical reference to the subclass. The
other control mixes a prepared path with the old compiled interface.
Ordinary dynamic import in that combination misses the method activation
and exits 70. Selecting ordinary loading up front preserves procedure
identity with the same mixed files. The tests distinguish these failures
from a valid closed scope.

** 2026-11-19 operational equivalence and counterexamples

The reachability proof requires the compiler to supply exact dependency
rows. Using the same rows to declare initialization equivalence would
leave their completeness untested. The =Effects= namespace defines a
separate reference interpreter for ordered initialization, observed values,
output, conditional failure and arbitrary unknown effects. The backward
projection derives required reads and writes from this execution model.
It accounts for cells that may be observed after normal return or after an
exception. The operational proof preserves recorded observations, their
order, the outcome, and those continuation-visible cells. A second proof
composes the projection with the prepared plan and its selection check.
An unproved observation or incompatible selection takes ordinary execution.

The test for incomplete scope deliberately removes a continuation
observation. The projection can then omit a write. Restoring the real
continuation adds the required write and matches ordinary execution.
Two entry stores with different live cells also give different traces
under identical selected artifacts. These controls keep the entry
state and observation assumptions visible. The compiler must establish
them from the actual binding and loader boundary.

The loader-result model reuses an already published success or exception.
The proposed admission guard distinguishes another worker's in-progress
load from a request by the current initializer itself. The installed
native loader control ran a failing initializer once, preserved its
first write, skipped the later write, and returned the identical exception
on repeat. A previous dynamic MCP source-control probe on the Homebrew
SDK exited 139 before an initialization receipt. This remains a failed
consumer observation.

Lean 4.34.1 checked the complete file with warnings treated as errors and
53 executable guards. Native counterexamples are associated with specific
proof obligations and checked against their stored receipts. Compiler
lowering still has to justify every classified operation, including
phase and MOP observations, native image grouping, and the initial
loader state. Actual manual-performance parity remains a separate
requirement.


The staging tempfile source was expanded with the available SDK. The
capture's destination resolves to the phase-zero binding owned by
=std/io/tempfile=. The allocator and environment reader resolve to
the runtime primitives =make-parameter= and =getenv=. A same-spelling
local =getenv= has a different defining module and fails this recognition.
This source witness establishes the call identities. The proof that
every classified native action implements its modeled effect remains
an integration obligation.

** 2026-11-19 check a physical plan against emitted images

The operational proof now constrains physical selection. The compiler
supplies an ordered image inventory, and each image owns an indivisible
action list. The checker projects the ordinary and proposed sequences
separately and compares the observable actions. Selection copies each
retained image whole in ordinary order. A mixed image can keep a private
definition alongside its required initialization. An unknown action, a
duplicate physical location, or a mismatched phase prevents admission.
The theorem =checked_images_refine_ordinary= proves the checked sequence
preserves the reference execution's observations, outcome and live state.
This is a compile-time check for a prepared artifact.

The installed compiler's own =generate-meta-code= supplies the origin
correspondence when expanding the current staging files. The sampled
modules are =std/io=, tempfile, socket/api, JSON reader, writer, I/O and
utilities, and LL1. The compiler reports an empty phase-zero body for
the =std/io= facade. A split-phase fixture demonstrates why the filename
number is insufficient. The writer emits =~1=, =~2= and =~3= as three
separate blocks in lexical phase 1. The third owns bindings in the nested
module but uses the outer module's native filename. The model stores
phase for binding semantics, and owner and optional block for physical
location. This distinguishes the wrapper from =~0= and rejects a physical
filename reused under a second logical phase.

In the fixture, the actual emitted Scheme for literal phase definitions
supplies the checked action lists. The lowering rejects an additional
unclassified output action. The generated Lean check selects block 2
and rejects a different block or phase. Native ordinary import opens
blocks 1, 2 and 3 and observes =late=. Executing the isolated checked
sequence opens block 2 and observes the same value. A later ordinary
import opens all three. Both paths reproduced the same receipts on
replay. The complete model has 60 executable guards, and the generated
artifact check adds three focused guards.

The executable work used the installed Homebrew SDK. The phase
selection is exercised through the isolated fixture. Normal compiler
and expander integration still needs to derive the demand and verified
effects for real consumer scopes. Performance against the original
five-import manual control remains open.

*** Independent qualification of a proposed physical scope

The proposal supplies a derivation witness. The reference supplies roots from
ordinary expansion, dependency rows and the ordinary image action inventory.
=ScopeCertificate.validate_exact= checks root coverage, closure and a
derivation for every entry. A missing row fails admission. The proof assumes
that the independent rows describe the real edges. Reusing the optimizer's
rows as reference would let an omitted initializer or an invented dependency
justify itself. The executable controls demonstrate both failures.

=qualified_images= checks the loader observation against the exact image set
and the ordered physical schedule. It also proves equality of visible effects,
failure and live state for the modeled actions. =qualified_matches_manual=
requires both implementations to pass the same independent reference before
deriving equality of their physical image sequences. Startup overhead remains
a measured quantity. The earlier manual narrowing changes when =tempfile=
captures =TMPDIR=, so that run supplies a latency reference with a semantic
difference. It cannot discharge the theorem's hypotheses.

Lean 4.34.1 accepted 77 guards in the model and six guards generated from the
phase observations. The model also exhausted all 512 directed graphs on
three vertices, eight root sets per graph, and eight candidate observed sets
per root set. A synchronous propagation oracle checked the 32,768 observation
comparisons. This bound includes cycles and checks both missing and extra loads.

The native facade used ordinary import and a reexported transformer. With the
Homebrew SDK, Gerbil =1cfb032= and Gambit =ea114fc=, both arms resolved the same
phase-zero syntax binding. The ordinary interface loaded both phase-one
objects at lookup. A temporary prepared interface loaded neither at lookup
and opened only =provider~2= on expansion. Both arms returned =73= twice.
The only differing emitted file is the provider's =.ssi=. The harness
explicitly loads the existing phase-zero body before evaluating the runtime
reference produced by the macro. The separate lookup-only run does not do
this and calls =syntax-local-value= before expanding anything. The ordinary
arm returns a procedure and the prepared arm returns =#f=. The replay finds
the defining phase block in the ordinary generated Scheme and rejects the
prepared observation at the public value read. The production design must
force the canonical transformer on a public value read or keep the ordinary
path when such reads cannot be intercepted. Name lookup stays separate.

*** Canonical binding publication and cross-image macro references

The public =syntax-binding-e= accessor and the unchecked accessor compile
to direct reads of slot 5 under =gxc -O=. The emitted calls to
=syntax-local-value= also use the specialized entry point. Updating the
getter alone cannot close this boundary. In the focused probe an ordinary
=syntax-binding= retains its ordinary =user-expander= object while the
payload waits on the real compiled procedure. Publishing the binding
fills that payload by using =load-module= before the handle reaches either
reader. Both optimized and ordinary readers see the same native procedure.
The binding, the expander, and repeated reads preserve object identity.
The provider's unused phase image stays absent. The direct-read control
sees a non-procedure through all three APIs.

Export and reexport registration, import collision checks, and name tests
need canonical identity. Public =resolve-identifier= and
=core-resolve-module-export= can hand a binding to compiled clients, so
those paths need a publication check before returning it. The internal
identity paths must remain separate to avoid eager activation during
registration or =free-identifier=?=. Raw context and table reflection can
bypass these functions. Until those paths are covered, opaque observers
require the ordinary phase path. The probe's explicit publication succeeds
without changing the representation of the checked and unchecked slot
accessors.

A second provider puts a helper, an unrelated macro, and a demanded macro
in three separate phase-one images. =generate-meta-code= enumerates those
images and =collect-expression-refs= identifies the demanded macro's
reference to the helper's canonical binding. Selecting only the macro's
own image returns a procedure and then exits 70 on expansion with an
unbound helper. Taking the closure selects the helper and macro in the
ordinary interface order. The native trace confirms that two images are
resident and the unrelated one is absent. Both complete expansions return
=73=. The unmodified reference opens all three. The Lean evidence consumes
these inventories, rejects the missing and extra images, and admits the
two-image footprint. The native observation is a presence snapshot, with
the ordinary order taken from the emitted interface.

The same collector read three exact staging source files on the Homebrew
compiler. The =:std/io= facade emits no separate phase block in this run.
=:std/io/socket/api= and =:std/format/api= emit one phase-one block each,
with eight and nine procedure definitions respectively. The unit for
physical omission is the whole existing image. The current evidence
qualifies shape and reachability in the focused cases. It supplies no
MCP or POO latency measurement.

It keeps the ordinary binding record and user expander. Internal

before returning a binding. The actual expansion method also forces the

binding directly and must run after publication. The first candidate
exposed a delayed payload to this predicate. The ordinary arm returned a

Gerbil MCP balance checks passed before and after the edits. Its syntax
runner failed in this environment. The checkout's =run.sh gxi= expanded
the three edited modules in their normal module context using the
available Homebrew SDK. The Homebrew compiler emitted optimized Scheme

declares its plan explicitly. Compiler emission and opaque context
reflection admission are separate open work.

All six ordinary and six candidate runs exited zero, observed the

offers deferral. The image check rejects it, and the new compiler emits

*** Preserving ordinary phase visits across module boundaries

A separately compiled consumer references the provider's canonical
phase-one helper. Its local closure contains its own image and omits the
provider image. Under the previous interface both consumer variants
failed with exit 70 when the provider was deferred, and both returned
=73= with the ordinary provider.

The compiler now retains every ordinary phase visit at its original
position in the emitted interface. Default import executes those visits
and binds the real native procedure. The closed-graph replay explicitly
selects deferral and the generated procedure descriptor activates its
required images on demand. The same compiled provider runs both modes.
All four default import combinations return =73=. All six public
binding and expansion routes pass in both modes. The closed plan's
physical order is =1,3= and the default order is =1,2,3=.

Explicit selection without a proof of the foreign edge still exits 70.
A consumer outside the precise scope that encounters a provider first
cached under deferral also exits 70. Thus the default protects the first
ordinary import, and the implementation still needs exact transitive
scope admission and cache-transition handling. Lean 4.34.1 accepts the
independent linked closure with the helper and rejects the same local
witness against the linked rows.

Fifteen staging standard-library modules compiled in the census, spanning
I/O, socket, JSON, JSON-RPC, formatting, iteration and errors. Zero
emitted a deferred interface. All eleven generated phase images contain
top-level syntax mark restoration. This is the observed limit of the
current lowering gate. No standard-library speedup is claimed from this
change.

the original module, phase, marks and captured origin identities.

All sixteen origin reads in the ordinary socket phase returned already

even if its nested route is ready. The final code rejected it. A second
control changed the public alias after capture. Late macro expansion
retained the original provider and origin identities. Lean 4.34.1 passed

All six cached transition regressions also passed in the preceding replay
of the new phase-context capture code.

*** Native counterexamples at the Lean qualification boundary

The reachability and effects proofs now compose with an independent native
replay in =NativeReplay.qualifyReplay=. The workload specifies which requests
and fields must be observed in order. Ordinary execution supplies the values.
The candidate supplies its own observed trace and physical image witness.
The theorem ties acceptance to the same complete observation sequence,
prescribed process exit, independently justified image set, and effect
projection. It assumes accurate canonical identities, dependency rows,
and observation coverage at the named boundary. Deriving those facts from
ordinary emitted code and independent loader observations remains part of
compiler qualification.

| Control | Observation preserved by the oracle | Result |
|---------+--------------------------------------+--------|
| Reexport lookup and public transformer read | The same binding must yield a procedure on the public read. | Rejects the earlier descriptor which returns =#f=. |
| Five hand-narrowed imports | JSON agrees. The =TMPDIR= capture moves from the earlier to the later value. | Rejects semantic parity for this workload. |
| Mixed socket interface | First listen succeeds. Method identity and exit status differ. | Rejects the stale candidate, which exits 70. |
| Published cached transformer | The prefix =[1, 3]= stays fixed and remaining visits run in order. | Recorded order is =[1, 3, 2, 4]=. |
| Failing initializer | One entry, same exception on repeat, first write retained, tail write absent. | Requires all observations. |
| Real TCP and named macro | Byte 73, provider and origin identities, reimport, and natural exit agree. | Passes the observed semantic projection. |
| Startup footprint and timing | Compare the exact image inventory and paired times. | Socket removes one image. Median paired time increases by 39.00 ms at import, 39.44 ms at receipt, and 95.12 ms at exit. |

Lean 4.34.1 passed with warnings treated as errors. The complete model has
77 prior executable guards. The new replay checks add 30 guards from
15 hashed native logs and controlled mutations, including shared missing
fields, truncated and reordered traces, missing exit, and extra images.
The native socket receipt checks use actual observations. The combined
physical-certificate positive control uses an independently specified
finite graph. The same-workload performance comparison against five
manual cuts remains open.

*** Independent native socket image replay

The ordinary emitted interfaces identify the reexport chain from =std/io=
through =std/io/api= to =std/io/socket/api=. The declared =tcp-connect=
runtime binding links to transformer =@tcp-connect=, whose closure is in
=std/io/socket/api~1=. The ordinary phase file and the candidate's phase
file are byte-identical. The lowered ordinary phase contains eight
closures, 16 serialized marks and 17 syntax-quote definitions. The two
nested mark contexts belong to =gerbil/core/contract=.

The original interface and the eager candidate load 230 images during
import. The deferred candidate loads 229 and internal canonical name
lookup leaves that inventory unchanged. Public transformer lookup
appends the omitted image. Ordinary uses rank 135, deferred uses rank
230, and the other relative ordering agrees. Compilation produces the
same expression and references the same four runtime globals, including
=std/io/socket/api#__tcp-connect=. Subsequent ordinary reimport leaves
the image inventory unchanged. All three processes exit zero.

Lean checks 14 observations and negative controls against a finite
reference inferred from these ordinary artifacts and native receipts.
The witness fixes all other images to test the elected phase edge.
It rejects premature phase loading, a missing named demand, and a
missing retained runtime body. Whole-graph minimality still requires
independent dependency rows for the other branches.

The allocate-on-first-visit candidate was measured separately in 12
pairs with the same TCP body and 229 loaded images in both arms.
Relative to the prior engine, paired medians rose by 126.63 ms for
import, 129.15 ms to the first receipt, and 226.87 ms end to end.
User CPU time rose by 65 ms and system CPU time rose by 15 ms. Both
run-order strata show a positive median change at the receipt. The
failed allocation candidate and its logs remain available for replay.

*** JSON reader closure and initialization boundary

The same native reader and socket objects were used for the ordinary
and automatic paths. Automatic pruning removed the two phase-one
images =std/encoding/json/reader~1= and =std/io/socket/api~1=. The retained
order agrees. Reader import loaded 244, 242 and 152 images for ordinary,
automatic and the five exact manual cuts respectively. At the first
JSON round trip the counts were 254, 252 and 197. The manual control
captures the later =TMPDIR= value.

In 12 matched triplets the automatic arm's paired median change at the
first receipt was -6.40 ms relative to ordinary and +330.54 ms relative
to manual. The ordinary comparison spans both signs. The manual arm is
faster in every pair. Peak RSS was lower than ordinary in all 12 pairs,
with a median paired saving of 581632 bytes. All JSON receipts agree
and each process exits zero.

The ordinary runtime image for =std/io/tempfile= combines the =TMPDIR=
capture, default file flags and callable definitions. The flags read
three expanded bindings from =std/os/file= and =std/os/fcntl=. A
diagnostic split using the existing loader slot initialized those two
definitions ahead of JSON import. It preserved the early directory,
parameter identity and flags while keeping the callable tempfile body
and random module out of the JSON receipt. The image count for this
projection was 209. Reopening the unchanged ordinary body reruns the
capture and replaces the parameter, so that control fails the semantic
check.

The isolated split also fails the public =load-module= contract. The
ordinary entry publishes the parameter and the callable body. An
initialization-only replacement publishes only the parameter. The
compiler plan needs distinct initialization and full activation demands
while preserving the ordinary entry. The design must prove the
ordered initializer schedule before emitting a cut and retain the
ordinary path on opaque dependencies.

Lean 4.34.1 checked 15 observations and controls from the raw receipts.
This experiment covers the specified JSON and tempfile behavior on the
Homebrew replay. No temporary probe is part of the Gerbil changes.

*** JSON Reader scope acceptance contract

The scope starts at the unchanged =std/encoding/json/reader= import,
retains =(import :std/io)= in that source, and measures the first
=[false,null,[true]]= round trip through the public JSON API. It carries
the =TMPDIR= observation through explicit later import and use. Native
fixtures also verify =parse-json= binding lookup, public transformer
publication, repeat import, and direct =load-module= activation.

[[file:../../models/build/JsonReaderScope.lean][JSON Reader Lean model]]
requires the ordinary runtime observations and the explicit positive
receipts. It distinguishes canonical binding demands from initialization
and physical image nodes. Each image beyond the manual comparison
requires a derivation from independently justified scope roots, and
every required image must occur at the measured boundary. Unknown rows
fail the footprint check. The manual import patch supplies the loading
and timing comparison. Ordinary execution supplies semantics.

| Boundary | Ordinary images | Automatic images | Manual images | Net automatic excess |
|----------+-----------------+------------------+---------------+----------------------|
| Reader import | 244 | 242 | 152 | 90 |
| First JSON receipt | 254 | 252 | 197 | 55 |

This replay checks all 12 paired trial identities with zero added
latency as the reference. The latency allowance is an explicit model
input. The current paired median excess is 519.279 ms at import and
330.538 ms at first receipt. The three automatic semantic comparisons
pass. The candidate has yet to qualify its footprint or reach latency
parity with the five manual cuts. Keep qualification on this workload
until the comparison closes.

*** JSON Reader per-image audit and executable edges

The matched Reader scope accounts for 91 automatic-only image identities
and one manual-only identity, giving the net 90 at the previous import
boundary. The ledger ties each identity to the selected binary and
lowered Scheme, with its observed load rank and importing context.
All 43 images observed outside the expander stack have paths through
ordinary emitted runtime loaders. A second trace sees all 43 through
real dynamic =__eval-import= calls at the same load ranks.
The Reader loader invokes =std/io=,
whose wrapper invokes =std/io/api=. The separate expander stack brings
36 additional identities through the socket facade. Reader's expanded
runtime uses the canonical =BufferedReader= and method bindings from
=std/io/interface/bio= and =std/io/bio/reader=. The ordinary tempfile
initializer still captures =TMPDIR= and reads file-flag bindings.

The scoped Lean model distinguishes visibility, binding, initialization
and evaluation-order edges. The proof preserves each executable edge
when constructing the execution table. Missing rows remain unknown.
The three Reader semantic fixtures check JSON and initialization,
the public =parse-json= transformer and repeat import, and direct
native tempfile activation.

- Ghuloum and Dybvig's library metadata, visit and invoke distinction
  motivates keeping name lookup and body demand separate.
  [[https://xivilization.net/~marek/tex/hellprog/papers/implicit-phasing.pdf][Implicit Phasing for R6RS Libraries, section 5]]
- Ferrante, Ottenstein and Warren's data and control edges motivate
  retaining the order and failure dependencies of evaluated forms.
  [[https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~pingali/CS395T/2009fa/papers/ferrante87.pdf][The Program Dependence Graph and Its Use in Optimization]]
- Flatt et al.'s linklet model makes the identity of mutable binding
  locations explicit across linking. The existing tempfile parameter
  identity is a concrete Gerbil observation of that requirement.
  [[https://doi.org/10.1145/3341642][Rebuilding Racket on Chez Scheme, section 3]]
- Waddell and Dybvig's separate-compilation analysis supplies the
  counterexample to deciding macro demand from visible names alone.
  [[https://legacy.cs.indiana.edu/~dyb/pubs/popl99.pdf][Extending the Scope of Syntactic Abstraction, section 5]]

The scoped compiler replay defers =std/encoding/base64~1=,
=std/io/tempfile~1= and =std/number/writer~1=. A fourth eligible module,
flock, remains ordinary after an isolated compiler link failure.
Native counts are 244 / 239 / 152 after import and 254 / 249 / 197 at
first receipt. Retained order and the three candidate semantic fixtures
agree. With 12 matched triplets the automatic paired median was
214.73 ms faster at import and 225.20 ms faster at receipt than ordinary,
with observed changes on both sides of zero. Against the manual arm
it was 1831.20 ms slower at import and 752.15 ms slower at receipt.
Manual won every comparison at both boundaries.

Replacing only the emitted native Reader wrapper left the dynamic
import and receipt inventories at 239 and 249. The exact reader
interface and bodies were held unchanged. The live evaluator emits
=__eval-import= and forces the module context through =__eval-module=.
The dynamic path therefore needs a validated execution table at that
context boundary. Changing only the AOT wrapper fails this workload
as an optimization.

The evaluator experiment keeps the ordinary =:std/io= binding import
and selects the existing runtime contexts for address types, hex,
format and address serde at the socket activation edge in dynamic
=__eval-import=. The selected descriptor identities and initializer
order were checked against the ordinary replay. Keeping only address
serde led to 212 objects at import and changed the order of retained
initialization. Retaining format without its earlier hex initialization
also changed that order. Both larger cuts are preserved as rejected
controls.

The admitted scope has 105 ordinary lowered phase-zero images. The
89 retained images have no recorded literal reference to the 16
deferred providers. Lean checks all 16 are from the 43 objects traced
through the dynamic evaluator, uses closure of the retained roots,
and checks that the candidate is exactly the ordinary load order
projected to retained objects. Tempfile still captures the earlier
=TMPDIR= with the same parameter object and flags. JSON, reader macro
reflection, repeat import and direct native tempfile activation pass.
A subsequent ordinary socket import restores the original dispatcher
and all 16 bodies. The native TCP receipt is 73 and the published
procedure and datagram class identities persist across repeat import.

*** JSON Reader dynamic format branch projection

The socket projection keeps the format facade in its retained group.
Within dynamic =__eval-import=, the next projection takes the existing
serde interface, serializer, deserializer, format environment and
=ioutil= contexts at the =std/format/api= activation edge. It preserves
the original =:std/io= source import and binding visibility. It defers
the five runtime bodies of format writer, reader, I/O, format and API.
The projected retained order agrees at reader import, first JSON
receipt and later tempfile demand.

The equivalent ordinary module source and native objects were checked.
Of 89 lowered runtime inputs, 88 match the previous replay by hash.
The remaining input is the installed number writer. The older
unoptimized replay overlay lacked the optimized integer entry points
that the installed =ioutil= and format objects call. Both old arms
failed with status 139 on numeric JSON and formatter tests. The
comparison restores the matching Homebrew provider, interface and
phase image to both automatic arms. The extra phase image makes the
comparable baseline 224 at import and 234 at receipt.

Lean checks the actual lowered rows. The 84 retained bodies have no
literal canonical reference into the omitted group. The JSON writer
and utility both demand =ioutil=. The stronger cut which delays it
loads 216 objects at import, then regains those three objects and
changes retained order at the first receipt. It fails qualification.
Attempting to delay the environment edge yields no additional cut.
All 12 semantic processes exit zero and Lean passes 24 checks before
timing. The added fixtures cover numeric and Unicode JSON, actual
formatter read and write, repeat import and the TCP continuation.
Later ordinary =:std/format= activation restores all five bodies and
preserves the published reader, class and parameter identities.

| Boundary | Comparable previous automatic | Format cut | Manual | Paired change versus previous | Paired change versus manual |

| First JSON receipt | 234 | 229 | 197 | +48.56 ms | +347.01 ms |

*** JSON Reader import expander cost breakdown

The Reader keeps its ordinary =:std/io= import. The control is the
previous automatic replay with 224 images at import. The rejected
socket-interface and format runtime cuts are disabled. There is no
new socket projection in this round. The source is staging
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= and the executable is
the patched Homebrew Gerbil =1cfb032= on Gambit =ea114fc=.
The separate full staging SDK build remains unqualified because
its OpenSSL headers were missing.

Seven alternating pairs retained the exact ordered image lists
before import, at Reader import, at the first JSON receipt, and
after the tempfile continuation, with zero exit status and the
expected JSON and TMPDIR receipts. The diagnostic build times the
active calls within the outer import form and classifies exclusive
time among the measured calls. =core-read-module= includes parsing.
Explicit reexport expansion times =export-imports= under =import:=
and is separate from all =core-find-module-export= binding lookups.
The binder time is exclusive of lookups. It includes =core-bind-import!=
construction and registration, with import-set assembly outside this
timer. Timed wrappers allocate and the rates include timer overhead.
The individual trace measurements leave other expander work
unattributed. The later uninstrumented benchmark measures end-to-end
latency separately.

| Instrumented segment (ms) | Automatic median | Manual median | Paired difference | Automatic calls | Manual calls |
|---+---+---+---+---+---|
| Read and parse interface | 61.01 | 24.47 | +34.70 | 104 | 53 |
| Explicit reexport expansion | 1.38 | 1.16 | +0.21 | 30 | 12 |
| Resolve exported binding | 25.19 | 11.73 | +10.98 | 97213 | 50593 |
| Construct and install import binding | 107.51 | 73.35 | +27.40 | 97213 | 50593 |
| Other export expansion | 18.38 | 10.59 | +6.82 | 278 | 138 |
| Native images during expansion | 244.17 | 108.09 | +130.67 | 93 | 51 |

The largest measured paired difference among these segments is
native image loading during expansion (about
131 ms).
The 42-image net non-runtime difference after the rejected socket
candidate consists of 43 automatic-only and one manual-only image.
All 43 automatic-only identities are observed as native load calls
during this retained automatic baseline. The largest group is
=std/format= at 30.75 ms across its phase images. The
individual maximum within those 43 is =std/serde/serialize~1= at
6.03 ms. =std/format/ioutil~1= accounts for
5.75 ms. The standard facade brings in additional
modules, affecting interface reads, binding installation and phase
loads together. Explicit reexport construction on its own accounts
for roughly one millisecond per arm.

Two direct omission controls fail in the original interfaces in the
first Reader import. The interface immediately evaluates the
=std/format/ioutil~1= export =@char->int= and the
=std/serde/serialize~1= export =do-write=. Omitting either phase load
produces the corresponding unbound-variable error and exits
70 or 70, before the JSON receipt. The existing
compiled interface requires these loads for its binding construction.
A change that separates name discovery from macro execution must
also model these early interface binding reads.

The manual arm intentionally captures the later TMPDIR at the

| Reader import | 224 | 152 |

*** JSON Reader format interface registration and transformer demand

This isolated experiment retains the ordinary =:std/io= import and
the preceding automatic baseline. It changes four interfaces in the
=std/format= branch and uses the original native phase and runtime
objects. Staging source is =dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=.
The replay uses patched Homebrew Gerbil =1cfb032= on Gambit =ea114fc=.

The previous omission failed because the installed =.ssi= evaluates
the native transformer reference while processing =%#define-syntax=.
Dropping only the =std/format/ioutil~1= load again reports an
unbound =@char->int= transformer and exits 70 before the JSON receipt.
The Lean model rejects that schedule. The separate name-registration
step can publish an unforced descriptor. Internal name lookup preserves
the phase state. A genuine use or public transformer reflection forces
the image once. The native evidence below checks those cases.

The generator reuses the compiler's lowered phase-origin classifier.
It recognizes procedures, local syntax-object construction and
hygienic marks with literal cache-checkable origins. Each rewritten
syntax binding is backed by a procedure in the original phase image.
The replacement uses the existing =load-compiled-phase-image= and
=make-compiled-phase-expander= mechanisms. The registry's existing
fallback requires an ordinary load when an origin cannot be resolved
from cached contexts. The selected image's phase-zero load stays in
its original location. =api= retains its positive-phase value
declarations and original load. =ascii=, =env=, =format-string= and
=format= keep their ordinary interfaces because the classifier does
not admit their lowered bodies.

The four changed interfaces are =ioutil=, =writer=, =reader= and =io=.
The two native export tables compare all 589 entries by name,
phase and defining binding. Candidate enumeration leaves all four
phase images unloaded. Actual =(@char->int #\I)= and its integer
case both return 73. The first call loads only =ioutil~1= among the four.
Public reflection in a separate process yields the original procedure
and forces earlier, as required. Repeat import preserves the binding
and transformer identities and the initialized tempfile parameter.
The original and candidate keep the same order of every retained
image at all four inventory boundaries. JSON, number and Unicode
round trips, macro compilation, renamed reexport, native tempfile
and real format read/write receipts pass in the three arms.

Lean 4.34.1 passed 38 checks, plus two general lemmas in the model.
The 24 workload-specific semantic processes and both export-table
processes exited zero before timing. The rejection above is a separate
counterexample. This validation covers the bounded JSON Reader path
and the observed initialization sequence.

| Loaded images | Automatic baseline | Format candidate | Five manual cuts |
|---+---+---+---|
| Reader import | 224 | 220 | 152 |
| First JSON receipt | 234 | 230 | 197 |
| Tempfile continuation | 234 | 230 | 213 |

The three-arm benchmark uses the same uninstrumented Reader workload
in 12 triplets, with each of the six orders executed twice. All 36
processes exited zero with expected receipts. The manual arm's later
TMPDIR capture happens after the first JSON receipt.

| Boundary | Automatic baseline median | Format candidate median | Five manual cuts median | Candidate minus baseline | Baseline minus manual | Candidate minus manual |
|---+---+---+---+---+---+---|
| Reader import | 1930.63 ms | 1843.04 ms | 1034.79 ms | -74.67 ms | +922.01 ms | +736.89 ms |
| First JSON receipt | 2047.50 ms | 2018.63 ms | 1627.32 ms | -52.78 ms | +512.20 ms | +361.29 ms |
| Process to exit | 3543.17 ms | 3361.95 ms | 2947.97 ms | -272.68 ms | +679.19 ms | +411.08 ms |
| User CPU | 1900.00 ms | 1890.00 ms | 1640.00 ms | -35.00 ms | +290.00 ms | +250.00 ms |
| System CPU | 385.00 ms | 365.00 ms | 275.00 ms | -20.00 ms | +100.00 ms | +80.00 ms |
| Peak RSS | 257.73 MiB | 254.50 MiB | 223.80 MiB | -3.27 MiB | +33.87 MiB | +30.59 MiB |

Reduction in the paired median gap to the manual arm (positive is
improvement) is +185.12 ms at Reader import and
+150.92 ms at the first JSON receipt. These are
differences between the two manual-referenced paired medians.
The direct candidate-minus-baseline import differences span
-1032.94 ms to +467.28 ms, with a lower candidate
time in 8 of 12 pairs. The receipt differences span

candidate time in 7 of 12 pairs.
The candidate remains above the manual latency gate.

*** JSON Reader format API phase-one helpers

The ordinary =:std/io= import and four previously admitted format
interfaces remain. This isolated replay changes only
=std/format/api.ssi= and keeps the installed native objects. The
source revision is =dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=, with
patched Homebrew Gerbil =1cfb032= running on Gambit =ea114fc=.

The lowered image contains two phase-one helper procedures,
=fold-format-string-for-stx= and =fold-format-ops-for-stx=. The
installed lowered sources and interface files reference their full
identities only in =api~1.scm= and =api.ssi=. Neither helper appears
among the API context's exports. The source retains both phase-one
runtime declarations and both positive-phase imports. The altered
interface registers seven syntax bindings through the existing
compiled-phase descriptors. It delays =api~1= while the original
phase-origin admission succeeds. The phase-zero load stays in place.

The private helpers read eagerly initialized values from
=std/format/format-string~0= and
=std/format/env~integer-conversions~0=. The original and candidate
load those two images in that order and before =api~0=. At all four
observed boundaries the candidate sequence is the exact projection
of the baseline with only =api~1= omitted. It preserves every
previously deferred format interface byte for byte.

The diagnostic native image traces the two helpers directly. No
call occurs before Reader import, during Reader import, through
the first JSON receipt or during name lookup. A dynamic format
pattern forces the transformer, with no call to either helper.
Literal =format= and =fprintf= each call the string helper and then
the operation helper, and return the receipt =73/(ok)=. The
ordinary and diagnostic native paths agree. Repeat import preserves
the binding, transformer procedure and tempfile parameter identities.

Three controls fail. Deleting the eager =api~1= load from the
ordinary interface exposes the immediate unbound =format=
transformer, with exit 70. Removing the format-string initializer
in the candidate reaches the first helper and terminates with
SIGSEGV. Removing the integer-conversion initializer reaches both
helpers and produces the integer-format-specifier error, with
exit 70. The preliminary controls that intercepted the global
loader missed the two compiled initializer calls. Their receipts
are retained and excluded from the evidence of failure.

Lean 4.34.1 passes 53 guards and the lookup lemma. The model rejects
a direct registration without the phase image, a premature private
helper read and missing or reversed initializer prerequisites.
Thirty original-object semantic processes and the two positive
instrumented processes exit zero before timing. The workload covers
actual literal and dynamic macro expansion, binding identity,
macro reflection, reexports, formatting, JSON and native tempfile.

|---+---+---+---|
| Reader import | 220 | 219 | 152 |
| First JSON receipt | 230 | 229 | 197 |
| Tempfile continuation | 230 | 229 | 213 |

| Combined | First JSON receipt | +337.43 ms | +314.24 ms | +23.19 ms | +2.05 ms |

boundaries in both cohorts and positive lower bounds for both
pooled 95 percent bootstrap intervals, resampling whole triplets.
The gap reduction fails the stated stability criterion.

*** JSON Reader admission before import expansion

The JSON Reader scope keeps =:std/io= and the original observed
initialization boundary. The prior phase-image experiments leave
the main source of work early in =core-import-module=, which opens
and expands an imported interface before publishing its context.
A fresh inventory-checked trace counted 104 interface reads on the
four-image baseline and 53 on the five manual cuts. Every one of
the 51 additional interface paths first passed through =:std/io=.
The first path through its socket API accounts for 37 of those
interface reads. The trace records the initial path and makes no
claim that the path is the only possible provider.

A diagnostic variant restored ordinary =:std/io/tempfile= at the
Reader import boundary of the manual arm. Its parameter, flags
(2562), callable and later observations equal the automatic
baseline on the checked tempfile fixture. Import and first-receipt
inventories are 168 and 213, compared with 152 and 197 for the
manual comparator and 220 and 230 for the automatic baseline.
This isolates the observed TMPDIR effect. Other visibility and
effect equivalence remain to be proved. No timing campaign was
run for this diagnostic variant.

[[https://xivilization.net/~marek/tex/hellprog/papers/implicit-phasing.pdf][Implicit Phasing for R6RS Libraries, sections 3.1 and 5]]
distinguishes transformer visits from invoking variable definitions
and initialization. The pinned Chez =41d5ffca7a07cbfc8807e719dedcc70ed74811cd=
keeps ordered import, visit and invoke requirements and separate
compile-time and runtime installations. The visit and invoke entry
points restore the pending operation after initialization failure.
[[file:../../vendor/chez/s/syntax.ss::2377][Chez descriptors]]
[[file:../../vendor/chez/s/syntax.ss::2478][Chez ordered execution]]
[[file:../../vendor/chez/s/compile.ss::710][Chez compiler emission]]

[[https://legacy.cs.indiana.edu/~dyb/pubs/popl99.pdf][Extending the Scope of Syntactic Abstraction, section 5.1]]
shows that a hygienic transformer can introduce a private read
or assignment into a separately compiled consumer. Gerbil's roots
must therefore carry actual expanded binding identities and phase.
[[https://home.dericilab.live/assets/docs/papers/icfp19-rrocs.pdf][Rebuilding Racket on Chez Scheme, section 3]]
makes dependencies explicit while keeping the underlying variable
locations attached to a particular instance. Gerbil can keep its
existing module registry and identity without replicating linklets.

The executable model extends =JsonReaderScope.lean=. Its input
records name lookups with their expected canonical binding,
expanded reads, actually demanded transformers, and required
initializers. It resolves requested reexport chains during
preparation and contributes no execution root for visibility
alone. It runs the existing least-closure worklist at import
and first receipt, including mandatory initialization roots.
It rejects a plan when a retained action lacks an ordinary
schedule entry or when the second boundary would insert an
action ahead of work already performed. The second boundary
contains only the additional actions. Admission at activation
takes the exact selected artifact identity and the prepared
schedule. It receives no export table or dependency graph.

Lean proves that accepted boundary actions are required, that
required scheduled actions remain, and that their order is
a subsequence of the ordinary schedule. Checks cover a forwarded
name, a socket name which stays visible without executing its
body, a demanded transformer, missing mandatory initialization,
an opaque observation and a stale artifact selection. This is a
prepared schedule model. The native emitter still needs to
derive exact dependency rows from expanded consumer artifacts
and enter the prepared path before recursive interface expansion.
Opaque, mixed or stale scopes use ordinary admission.

*** Five manual imports in the Gerbil source PR

The exact five-file manual patch tracked by Issue 2 was applied to
Gerbil PR 1 on parent =d2fbaf0=. All five before and after files match
the previously compiled comparison inputs byte for byte. The MCP
balance calls succeeded on both source versions. The standalone
syntax tool lacks module context for the relative =./env= import.
Checkout-local =run.sh gxi= expanded each complete module, and the
existing MOP and JSON suites passed against both native overlays.

One scoped POO run imports the object module and public JSON API,
reads a slot, and checks the first JSON round trip. The installed
POO prefix failed on both arms due to mixed bindings. A shared
isolated native POO artifact set replaced that prefix. All 16
timed launches returned 73 and exited zero. The automatic
process harness and timeout were the same on both sides.

| Workload | Original median | Narrowed median | Paired median savings |
|----------+-----------------+-----------------+----------------------|
| POO slot read and JSON round trip | 2856.78 ms | 1407.45 ms | 1059.25 ms |

The narrowed arm was quicker in all eight pairs. The MOP run
establishes functional compatibility. The plain manual JSON
control still initializes tempfile at explicit import and
therefore observes the later TMPDIR. This source-level change
and the prepared startup model remain independently qualified.

*** Native JSON Reader prepared expansion input checkpoint

The Reader source and compiled ordinary import retain =:std/io=.
The isolated expander adds a prepared input hook immediately before
=core-read-module= in the existing =core-import-module= path. It still
uses the ordinary module registry, scope construction, expansion,
and evaluator. Explicit reload and unprepared requests use the
ordinary input path.

A preparation run captures actual input reads before Reader import
and before the first JSON receipt. These phase boundaries are
selected by =JsonReaderScope.prepareReaderStartup= and consumed in
native gxi. Ten actual I/O binding identities from the installed
lowered JSON and LL1 artifacts and the established early tempfile
initializer were added to the source-bound plan. Adding those
roots produces exactly the same emitted interface selection.
Its underlying closure retains the observed ordinary edges where
complete effect and macro demand rows are unavailable.

The selected artifact stores portable syntax with its original
source positions. Admission compares the selected load path and
original interface source bytes before installing either stage.
The measured process includes artifact decoding and byte validation.
The native hook consumes 103 prepared inputs at Reader import and
seven additional inputs at the JSON stage. The base prelude input
still goes through the ordinary reader. A substituted source-byte
receipt makes admission fall back before any planned input is used.

The module balance and syntax checks and complete checkout-local
expansion passed. There are 18 Lean guards for preparation and
native receipts, and 27 zero-exit native semantic processes before
timing. The fixtures check the Reader transformer, repeated import,
actual formatting, native tempfile publication, and a dynamically
constructed import after the receipt. That import resolves the
same procedure under the facade and under its owner. The TMPDIR
capture and parameter identity agree with the ordinary automatic
baseline. All four ordered physical image inventories match exactly.

This path removes repeated parsing of admitted input forms but still

*** JSON Reader pruning plan audit

The target is parity with the five manual imports in elapsed import
and first JSON receipt. The ordinary open-import execution supplies
the semantic reference. Keep the source =:std/io= import and use
the exact manual source files from Gerbil PR 1 as the time comparison.

**** What accounts for the gap

In the last matched run, the four-image automatic baseline was
481.74 ms slower than manual at import and 246.85 ms at first
receipt. The serialized input variant was 590.62 ms and 397.16 ms
slower than manual. The direct paired regressions against the
same automatic baseline were 88.95 ms and 100.68 ms. These are
independent medians of paired differences.

The frontier trace found 104 interface reads on the automatic arm
and 53 on the manual arm. All 51 additional interfaces first enter
through =:std/io=. The path via the socket API accounts for 37 of
those first reads. The same edge accounts for 29 of 39 automatic-only
native image loads during expansion. First discovery through an edge
does not establish that the edge is the module's only live provider.

The earlier expander profile found 97,213 import binding installations
against 50,593. In that cohort, measured paired overhead was 130.67 ms
for native loads during expansion, 34.70 ms for interface reads,
27.40 ms for binding installation excluding lookups, and 0.21 ms
for explicit reexport expansion. These segmented measurements describe
another cohort and leave other work unattributed. They do not allocate
the end-to-end gap to individual branches. Avoiding reexport dispatch,
serializing the input syntax, or deferring one phase image cannot
by itself remove the repeated expansion and binding work.

Ordinary and prepared inventories agree at 220 images at import
and 230 at receipt. The manual counts are 152 and 197. A manual
diagnostic that restores early tempfile activation gives 168
and 213 and reproduces the observed TMPDIR and parameter behavior.
That diagnostic isolates one known initialization requirement. It
establishes neither a minimum safe inventory nor a latency budget.

[[file:/Users/guangtao/ghq/github.com/tao3k/gerbil-bazel/.data/gambit-SMP/.cache/tasks/repair-9-12/runs/json-reader-native-plan.Yw1CVmka/paired/summary.json][Matched automatic, prepared and manual wall time]]
[[file:/Users/guangtao/ghq/github.com/tao3k/gerbil-bazel/.data/gambit-SMP/.cache/tasks/repair-9-12/runs/json-reader-startup-plan.zccodmD1/frontier-summary.json][First interface paths and tempfile control]]
[[file:/Users/guangtao/ghq/github.com/tao3k/gerbil-bazel/.data/gambit-SMP/.cache/tasks/repair-9-12/runs/json-reader-expander-cost.EVQe5Jq3/breakdown-summary.json][Exclusive expander work]]

**** Work in dependency order

1. Make the compiler produce the dependencies. After ordinary expansion,
   emit a canonical binding and phase index from the existing metadata
   path in =compile-meta-code= and =generate-meta-code=. Include private
   references introduced by hygienic macros, actual transformer visits,
   positive-phase value providers, and separately justified initializer
   roots. A facade alias carries the defining binding identity and weak
   or strong import behavior. Check import conflicts during ordinary
   preparation even if a conflicting name is absent from the read roots.
   Close both Reader import and first receipt with independently derived
   rows. The observed read tree and the ten lowered I/O bindings are
   evidence inputs. The read tree cannot certify its own completeness
   or label every extra read a required effect.

2. Skip branch expansion at the existing entry point. Admit the
   prepared artifact before =core-expand-module-begin= in
   =core-import-module=. Resolve only demanded names into their
   defining providers. Keep export visibility and the existing loader's
   module instances. With a closed scope, avoid opening, expanding and
   binding the unnecessary interfaces below the broad import. Audit
   the structural reduction at the first =:std/io= edge, including
   paths first discovered via the socket facade. Retain any provider
   reached by an alternate binding, macro or effect edge. This is a
   branch-level change. Leave the prior one-image experiments archived.

3. Retain the effect spine and phase ordering. Derive the ordered
   actions from ordinary =__eval-import= and use the existing loader
   state. Early TMPDIR capture, file flags, method and prototype
   registration, positive-phase value initialization and failure order
   are separate obligations. An unsplittable image remains eager.
   A split initializer must preserve the same published locations
   and their identity when later explicit import or direct
   =load-module= activates the rest of the body. A dormant pure
   action may move across the receipt boundary only with a checked
   absence of an ordering obligation. Unknown requirements select
   ordinary handling before planned effects. A later dynamic request
   must use the existing initialized state without replaying it.

4. Keep the native startup schedule compact. Emit the two ordered
   action deltas and exact artifact identities at preparation time.
   Startup admission must not deserialize the ordinary interface
   graph, reread every planned interface to authenticate it, or
   run the general dependency worklist. Use the owning loader's
   exact artifact selection, with validation and plan decoding in
   the measured process. The installed expander and runtime keep
   their current responsibilities. An opaque or stale selection
   falls back through the same owners.

**** Acceptance and rollout

- Lean checks least closure for compiler-derived rows, canonical binding
  and phase identity, missing and stale rows, opaque eval, required
  effects, exact ordered effect projection and once-only continuation.
  The native producer must attest the actual rows and artifacts. Soundness
  of a supplied table alone does not qualify its compiler producer.
- Native semantic tests compare against the unmodified open import.
  Cover Reader macros, a macro introducing a private binding, renamed
  reexports and strong collisions, literal and dynamic format calls,
  computed eval after the first receipt, repeated import, early TMPDIR,
  and direct native tempfile publication. Retain the missing-transformer
  and missing-initializer controls. Then check actual interface paths,
  action counts and order. Require evidence of skipped recursive work
  before investing in another timing round.
- Use the unchanged JSON workload and time boundaries, with exact
  source and SDK identity and matched automatic, candidate and
  manual processes. Include admission, decoding and actual
  initialization in elapsed time. Report paired timing, memory,
  phase/image counts and both boundary receipts. The parity target
  is zero added paired median latency against the same-run manual arm
  at both boundaries. Repeat in reverse order when judging stability.
  If extra mandatory work prevents parity, identify the retained
  actions and measure their cost. Continue to report the raw manual
  gap under the same endpoint; the tempfile diagnostic does not
  replace the manual arm.
- Keep the five-import PR improvement available while the compiler
  path qualifies. Require Reader parity and its semantics before
  making an automatic replacement. Expand the scope to POO, MCP
  and the wider module system only after the focused gate closes.

*** Compiler-produced JSON Reader dependencies and socket order counterexample

The native replay used Gerbil staging source =dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=
on the patched Homebrew Gerbil =1cfb032= SDK. The two native inventory arms
selected the same installed interfaces and images. The comparison here uses
ordinary evaluation and the previous experimental socket deferral. GitHub
reported =4de7a63ba11c28d2ceb40b359b79c035fe0cf3c0= as the head of the
five-import PR. No new comparison was timed.

**** Compiler output

The Reader source and its emitted interface both retain =:std/io=.
The isolated compiler records user transformer applications at
=core-apply-user-expander= and emits references and the lowered commands
at the existing runtime and metadata compilation boundaries. Each runtime
or positive-phase block records the canonical references, original form
order and generated loader. The capture covers 29 staged source modules
including the Reader, JSON receipt implementation, all ten direct I/O
facade children, and the principal socket and format witnesses. The
startup capture records actual native image load order independently.

Positive compiler witnesses include =BufferedReader::interface= and the
BIO UTF-8 read and peek bindings. The actual Reader transformer providers
include =std/io/interface/bio= and =std/io/bio/reader=. The compiled
receipt modules also reference =std/io/util='s buffered helpers.
The =std/io/api= loader emits ten direct children in declaration order.
The =std/io/socket/api= loader emits =std/io/socket/types=, whose runtime
image defines the datagram type via =make-class-type=. The Reader's
direct binding and transformer rows have no socket provider. At positive
phase, the =std/format/api= references reach =std/format/format-string=
and =std/format/env~integer-conversions=. The tempfile block contains
the parameter allocation with =TMPDIR= capture and the file flags.

The compiler output covers observed expanded references and emitted
actions. It does not yet give a closed certificate for every export,
phase origin, publication, exception, or native opaque effect. Four
additional facade children (=dummy=, =counter=, =delimited= and
=detachable=) and =file= have eager or opaque lowered forms. Their
omission has no certificate from this run.

**** Native checkpoint and Lean replay

| Arm                       | Before import | After Reader | First JSON receipt | Datagram descriptor before computed eval |
|---------------------------+---------------+--------------+--------------------+------------------------------------------|
| Ordinary open import      |            54 |          244 |                254 | present                                  |
| Earlier socket deferral   |            54 |          220 |                230 | absent                                   |

Both arms returned =[false,null,[true]]= and the actual =@char->int=
transformer returned 73. Both retained early =TMPDIR= and the same
temporary-directory parameter. The ordinary arm had already published
the datagram class before the computed =eval= import. The deferred arm
only published it on that later import. Ordinary loading orders the
socket types and API before dummy, file and tempfile. The deferred arm
loads the socket images after all three. The format-string and integer
conversion images remain in order in both arms. Counts are total named
native images at the checkpoint, including the common 54-image starting
inventory. The smaller arm fails equivalence for the class observation.

The existing Lean startup model was rebuilt with Lean 4.34.1 and the
evidence replay passed. It accepts the observed ordinary prefix and
rejects the prefix missing the early native class and API. The observed
later publication also fails the ordinary order check. Missing and opaque
rows take the ordinary path. This projection checks the witnessed
requirements and does not certify completeness of the emitter's input.

The ordinary replay receipts also pass for Reader macro use, renamed
reexport, numeric JSON, direct native tempfile publication, and format
transformer invocation and reflection. There is no admitted early
recursive cut from this evidence, so the manual performance gate was
not run. The next compiler change needs a way to retain the same native
publication actions in their early order while avoiding recursive
interface expansion for the unused name path. A later load of the
same native images cannot recover an observation that already happened.
