*** JSON Reader socket initialization and interface split

The native candidate kept =(import :std/io)= on staging source
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= and used the same patched Homebrew Gerbil =1cfb032= SDK as
the controls. The scoped admission runs before =core-expand-module-begin=
and matches the Reader ancestry and the actual socket API artifact.
It supplies the socket facade's compiled declarations and the existing
runtime load commands separately. The operation leaves the socket types
on the ordinary path and runs the seven private runtime images in
their ordinary positions before the socket API image. The existing
=load-module= registry owns those images and reuses their publication
when the regular interface is later demanded.

Preparation checks the native loader's order, the prerequisite closure
of the retained imports, the allowed interface forms, compiler-lowered
phase initialization and the actual public transformer block. Each
omitted phase block uses only local syntax construction, transformer
procedures and its recorded mark origins. The API's own phase block and
the shared format and address dependencies remain on the original path.
This is a scoped replay over the pinned artifacts. Production admission
for edited artifacts and compilation outside this Reader scope still
require a source-bound implementation.

**** Structural and semantic results

| Checkpoint and inventory | Ordinary | Socket split | Manual imports |
|--------------------------+----------+--------------+----------------|
| Reader interface reads | 104 | 96 | 53 |
| Reader native images | 244 | 237 | 152 |
| First receipt interface reads | 111 | 103 | 78 |
| First receipt native images | 254 | 247 | 197 |

The split avoids the socket API interface and seven private interfaces:
=sockaddr=, =socket=, =basic=, =client=, =stream=, =server= and
=datagram=. The compact projection itself is read once. Exactly seven
phase images are absent at both checkpoints. Removing those seven
from the ordinary native sequence yields the candidate sequence,
including the other positive-phase loads and all runtime images.
Lean 4.34.1 checks that order and rejects a missing class image,
a reordered startup, and an opaque boundary.

The datagram class is already published at the Reader checkpoint.
Computed =eval=, explicit datagram import and repeated =:std/io= imports
retain the same class and socket procedures. Both native arms retain the
ordinary =TMPDIR= value and temporary-directory parameter. Ten paired
semantic fixtures matched, including direct native publication, numeric
JSON, real Reader and format transformers, reexport identity, all eight
socket procedure transformers, and a TCP loopback round trip returning 73.

**** Paired performance

The manual arm uses the five narrowed source files at
GitHub PR 1 head =a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=. Those files are byte-matched to
the previously qualified artifacts. GitHub's compare from
=4de7a63ba11c28d2ceb40b359b79c035fe0cf3c0= lists changes only to linked build support, the
compiler driver and the AOT build command. All arms use the same SDK
and instrumentation. The timing includes the candidate's projection
read, covers 12 matched triplets in both traversal orders, and uses
the unchanged JSON input and 180 second process watchdog. All 36
processes completed naturally with identical JSON receipts.

| Elapsed wall time in ms | Ordinary median | Socket split median | Manual median | Socket split − ordinary paired median | Socket split − manual paired median |
|-------------------------+-----------------+---------------------+---------------+----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------|
| Reader import | 1281.20 | 1413.15 | 724.98 | -33.85 | +616.98 |
| First JSON receipt | 1364.73 | 1516.21 | 1118.53 | +2.40 | +425.97 |

- Reader import, forward / reverse paired deltas against ordinary +372.22 / -86.04 ms, 7/12 faster and manual +2097.66 / +592.53 ms, 0/12 faster.
- First JSON receipt, forward / reverse paired deltas against ordinary +368.32 / -79.37 ms, 6/12 faster and manual +1061.39 / +396.89 ms, 0/12 faster.

The candidate remains slower than the manual paired median at both checkpoints.
Matched peak RSS changes were -2.86 MiB against ordinary and +26.87 MiB against manual. The split used less peak RSS than ordinary in all 12 pairs.
Wall-clock deltas relative to ordinary change sign between the forward and reversed blocks. The startup reduction therefore qualifies structurally and semantically, with unstable latency benefits.
The manual comparator publishes the datagram class and captures =TMPDIR= later than the ordinary open import. Its timing is retained as the requested performance comparator.

**** Acceptance decision and remaining startup cost

Rejected for performance. The socket split remains an isolated
semantic control and is not a candidate for adoption. Relative to
ordinary, the paired import delta changed from +372.22 ms to -86.04 ms
between the forward and reversed blocks. The first-receipt delta
changed from +368.32 ms to -79.37 ms. Against manual, the split lost
all 12 pairs at both boundaries.

Computing the post-import interval within each process shows the split
was 299.37 ms faster than manual in paired median over that interval.
It begins the interval 616.98 ms behind in paired median. These
independently computed medians are not additive. The largest remaining
opportunity is before Reader import returns.

Joining the current eight omitted interfaces to the earlier
first-discovery trace leaves 43 extra reads relative to manual.
Of these, 29 first entered via the socket API and 27 of those
followed its =:std/net/address= import. First discovery attributes
opening work and does not establish necessity or exclusive ownership.
Additional leaf deferrals under socket are no longer the target.
The next admission decision needs to separate address and format
provider visits with observed phase or initialization duties from
recursive name expansion that Reader does not use.

*** Revised JSON Reader design for the address expansion path

Keep the Reader source import =:std/io= and use the existing manual
PR as the performance comparator. The seven-leaf socket split is a
semantic control. The first-discovery trace locates 27 of the 43
remaining excess interface reads under =:std/net/address=. The
largest path runs through address string conversion into
=:std/string/stringer= and =:std/format=. Measure whether a compact
initialization path can remove this recursive expansion in one change.

1. In ordinary compilation, record canonical binding keys and phases
   after expansion, actual transformer calls and immediate phase
   evaluations, and the ordered initialization actions from the
   runtime loader. Follow a reexport to its defining binding. Keep
   exported spellings and import conflict checks in the existing
   expander. Transformations which read an opaque value preserve the
   ordinary path. Keep three separate reasons for reaching a provider:
   a binding, a phase evaluation, or a startup effect.

2. Attach the checked result to the exact build. Use the compiler's
   existing metadata and runtime loader paths to emit a compact
   selection for Reader import and a second delta before its first
   receipt. A facade's unchanged name table refers to canonical
   providers. The initialization path names existing native images
   when they already cover the effects. When a required action shares
   an indivisible image with unrelated code, retain that image first.
   A future split needs its own proof of publication and startup order.
   Emit no list of names learned only from the benchmark trace.

3. Admit the compact selection before =core-expand-module-begin=.
   Validate the selected build identity and scope before any action,
   without reopening every skipped interface. Dispatch initializers
   through the existing evaluator and native loader so publication,
   failure and once-only behavior survive reuse. Materialize an
   interface on actual name, transformer or dynamic eval demand,
   using the same module instance and canonical binding identities.
   Ordinary behavior is the fallback before executing a planned action.

4. Build the Lean fixture from the actual two-boundary compiler output.
   The model must relate interface materialization to the same
   provider instance already used by initialization. Compare the
   projected ordinary effects in order, including identities and
   failure prefixes. Keep the address class and datagram publications,
   the address Stringer and resolver methods and the untaint
   registration as effect witnesses. Check the immediate
   =@char->int= evaluation and the format provider order. Regressions
   must include a missing effect, reordered providers, duplicate
   publication on computed eval, and an incompatible artifact.

5. In the isolated Reader replay, first verify the actual bindings,
   macros, dynamic eval, address registration and initialization order.
   Then compare the initialization-only diagnostic with the full
   candidate to price the retained work and the admission overhead.
   Count actual reads and native loads before either timing gate.
   Measure prepare/build cost separately. Include admission, loading
   and initialization in the paired wall clock, with original and
   exact PR manual arms. Require a stable result in both execution
   orders and parity at both endpoints before widening scope. Use an
   effect-matched manual diagnostic to explain a remaining gap while
   retaining the raw PR comparison as the gate.

*** Research-based design for scoped import pruning

**** OCaml source correspondence

The vendored OCaml checkout reports version 5.5.1 at
=46e1c324fb9e9f21f52c9cc3399020f7ef8b9b91=. For this study the source
distinguishes three decisions.

- The persistent environment imports compiled interfaces and checks their
  digests. Native unit metadata separately records interface imports,
  implementation imports, definitions, and whether the unit must be linked.
- Native =scan_file= includes an explicit =.cmx= unconditionally. For an
  archive it scans unit metadata in link order and keeps a member when it
  satisfies a requirement, has =ui_force_link=, or the caller used
  =-linkall=. A selected unit contributes its own requirements.
  =Linkdeps= reports missing and wrongly ordered units. Bytecode does
  analogous selection from archive metadata with its own relocation and
  required-unit information. The native linker emits =caml_program= with
  calls to the selected units' entry points in order.
- Flambda's pass walks the program body backwards from its result symbol,
  draws dependencies from free symbols and computes a fixed point for
  recursive definitions. If a symbol is dead, an effectful initializer
  field remains as an explicit =Effect= node. This is below compilation
  unit selection. Dynlink checks the plugin and existing unit state before
  invoking its entry points and publishing the loaded state. It supplies
  a separate path for runtime extension.

[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/typing/persistent_env.ml::121][Persistent interface digests]]
[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/file_formats/cmx_format.mli::38][Native unit metadata]]
[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/asmcomp/asmlink.ml::160][Native archive member selection]]
[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/bytecomp/bytelink.ml::112][Bytecode unit requirements]]
[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/utils/linkdeps.ml::53][Required and missing units]]
[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/asmcomp/cmm_helpers.ml::2598][Ordered startup entry points]]
[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/middle_end/flambda/remove_unused_program_constructs.ml::41][Symbol closure and effect retention]]
[[file:../../vendor/ocaml/otherlibs/dynlink/dynlink_common.ml::341][Explicit runtime extension]]
[[https://ocaml.org/manual/5.4/flambda.html][OCaml manual on effects and coeffects]]

**** Why the Chez-inspired experiment had little benefit

Ghuloum and Dybvig's expanded library comprises metadata, pre-expanded
visit code, and pre-expanded invoke code. A macro reference can trigger
a visit, which can itself require initialized variables from another
library. Chez keeps separate visit and invoke requirements and tracks
completion and resets per operation. The compiler can use the phase
information before choosing which implementation to activate.

In the current Gerbil path, =core-expand-import%= asks =import-module=
for module contexts which recursively expand interfaces, and then
installs their import bindings. Some positive-phase imports also
evaluate a module while expanding. Deferring a phase block after these
steps retains the recursive name and context work. The socket split
avoided eight interface reads and seven phase objects, with the complete
native runtime order preserved. It still paid for the address umbrella
and most of the format and serialization expansion. The prepared
projection also had a real read and admission cost. Paired wall-clock
deltas against ordinary changed sign across order blocks. Available
measurements do not isolate a unique cause for the wall-clock variation.

The earlier profile found 97,213 binding installations for the automatic
path and 50,593 for manual. The paired native loading overhead during
expansion in that cohort was 130.67 ms. The 27 remaining first reads
through =:std/net/address= identify where to look for closure-level
work. Their discovery path supplies neither a proof of dead code nor
the cost of their removal. The manual narrowing also changes observable
startup publication, which is why its time is a target and ordinary
evaluation is the semantic reference.

[[https://xivilization.net/~marek/tex/hellprog/papers/implicit-phasing.pdf][Implicit Phasing for R6RS Libraries, sections 5.1–5.3]]
[[https://legacy.cs.indiana.edu/~dyb/pubs/popl99.pdf][Hygienic binding identity across separate compilation]]
[[https://home.dericilab.live/assets/docs/papers/icfp19-rrocs.pdf][Phase-level linklets and shared binding locations]]
[[file:../../vendor/chez/s/syntax.ss::2478][Chez visit and invocation lifecycle]]

**** Scope, algorithm, and the engineering gates

A scope names the exact build and loader selection, the already
resolved consumer, the observation boundaries, and code which may
introduce new demands. The initial scope is the standard JSON Reader
import and first JSON receipt with ordinary =:std/io= visibility.
A computed load whose target cannot be derived must use the existing
resolver. An unknown operation before the scoped boundary invalidates
admission unless ordinary behavior of that operation can be retained
within the plan.

1. Emit three inputs from the existing compiler. Export metadata maps
   spelling and phase through reexports to the owning binding and
   preserves versioned conflict semantics. Expanded references and
   actual transformer dependencies describe executable binding use,
   including private references introduced by hygienic expansion.
   Lowered code records ordered evaluation units, effects and reads
   of mutable or external state. Native, computed or opaque actions
   require conservative handling. Traces independently falsify the
   input rows and never establish their completeness.
2. Seed an indexed worklist with live value uses, demanded transformer
   visits and mandatory initialization events. Visit nodes by owner,
   phase and action or binding identity. Follow only semantic
   dependency edges and terminate recursive groups at a fixed point.
   A name lookup alone can resolve a provider through metadata. Each
   executable provider still needs physical coverage by its exact
   artifact. When publication and dead code share an indivisible
   image, keep the image until independent publication is proven.
3. Project the retained actions onto ordinary order at the two native
   checkpoints. Emit the prefix and its first-receipt delta with the
   compiler's loaded artifact identities. Perform closure construction
   and verification before startup. Admit the compact result before
   recursive interface expansion. Use the existing runtime and phase
   owners to preserve publication once, reuse and original failure
   order. A later dynamic request can materialize its interface
   against the same module instance.
4. Start with the address umbrella. Track the actual address types,
   Stringer and resolver methods, serde untaint registrations and
   immediate format phase evaluations. Compare ordinary evaluation,
   a checked initialization schedule used to price the retained work,
   and the compiler candidate. Test demand for renamed exports and
   hygienic private bindings, =@char->int=, failing initialization,
   computed and repeated import, and stale artifact or module state.
5. Extend the existing Lean worklist proof to cover the exact emitted
   rows, valid artifact ownership, ordered effect prefixes on success
   and failure, and continuation against the same initialized instance.
   Test small generated graphs with reexport cycles, a missing row,
   and phase or registration dependencies without a value edge, using
   exhaustive reachability as a reference. The native replay must
   independently check the predicted reduced expansion and identity.
6. Record actual recursive reads, binding installations, positive-phase
   visits, native initialization and the cost of admitting the plan
   before timing. Keep the unchanged ordinary and exact PR manual
   comparator in paired end-to-end runs. The same design may apply
   to other libraries. Rollout beyond Reader requires equivalent
   observations and parity at both Reader timing boundaries.

[[https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~pingali/CS395T/2009fa/papers/ferrante87.pdf][Program dependence, data and control edges]]
[[file:../../models/build/RuntimeImportDemand.lean::317][Existing worklist and closure theorem]]
[[file:../../models/build/JsonReaderScope.lean::350][Existing ordered two-stage startup model]]

*** Selected design and first Lean stage

Select build-time demand analysis with a verified physical startup
schedule for each admitted consumer scope. Emit provider and export
metadata for independent compilation. Keep the existing expander and
loader as the owners of binding resolution and publication. The startup
path reads a compact plan before it recursively expands the interfaces
in that plan's scope.

| Approach | Decision and trade-off |
|----------+------------------------|
| Hand-narrowed imports | Keep the qualified PR as a timing reference and an independently useful optimization. Narrowing can change early registrations and environment capture. |
| Deferring individual phase images | Reuse as a local mechanism where its declaration is proved. The Reader result showed that modest footprint reductions leave most shared expansion costs. |
| Build-time demand and physical startup schedule | Chosen for the automatic path. Resolves executable demand once and validates which whole native images can deliver it in ordinary order. Adds compiler metadata and supports independent phase analysis. |

The graph's nodes are canonical bindings, phase evaluations and ordered
initialization actions. Derive least closure for an exact consumer and
build. Map that closure to the native images which can execute it.
An initializer can require an image whose exported definitions have
no consumer in the scope. Retain that image until a finer partition
satisfies the same publication and failure contract. Worklist closure
and effect validation happen at build time. Startup selects the exact
already validated plan, executes it using the existing owners, and
allows later demand to use the same binding locations. A stale selection
or opaque obligation takes the ordinary path before executing effects.

The first Lean stage now composes logical demand with ordered native
image checks in =JsonReaderScope.lean=. Every scheduled request must
have an image provider. For each boundary the validated physical
projection preserves the model's ordinary observations and failures.
The first-receipt delta requires the chosen import sequence to be an
unchanged prefix, including the action list of each retained image.
The model controls exercise preserving an address initializer, dropping
an unused phase image, admitting a later writer image, and rejecting
missing delivery, missing registration, reordered execution and changed
image actions. The Reader compiler and socket replay models also pass
on Lean 4.34.1. The new fixture is symbolic. Native artifact ownership,
effect completeness and actual wall time still need compiler and
native evidence.

The next implementation starts with =:std/net/address= in the ordinary
JSON Reader import scope. First derive the actual image and action rows
from the existing compiler and loader. Attach the plan to the installed
build before recursive expansion. Use the unchanged ordinary path to
validate type, method and serde publication, format phase demand, and
dynamic eval identity. Measure the cost of retaining that initialization
separately from interface expansion and admission. If the required
work itself exhausts the manual timing budget, the evidence can locate
which physical image needs a finer partition. The raw PR #1 pairing
remains the final gate for import and first receipt. Require stable paired parity at both boundaries.

*** Address runtime schedule and phase admission

The Git Cons PR 1430 head and the isolated source both select
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=. The native run uses
the existing patched Homebrew SDK with the staged compiler replay overlay.
The JSON Reader source retains =:std/io=. A compiler observer re-expanded
and lowered nine staged modules across address, Stringer and format.

The isolated preparer follows the selected compiler-emitted native loader
wrappers in order. It rejects non-loader commands in a wrapper. It pairs
a copied binary with source in another directory only when the two
binaries are byte-identical. The schedule covers 73 loader wrappers
and 63 native bodies. Thirty-nine bodies are already loaded when
socket initialization reaches address. The remaining 24 execute in
ordinary order. Native replay runs them through the existing loader at
the original address evaluation point.

The ordinary and prepared arms have identical ordered native
checkpoints with 244 images at Reader import and 254 at first receipt.
They return the same JSON and address binary serde round trips. Both
publish the address and datagram classes, Stringer and resolver methods,
and serde untaint method before first receipt. Computed eval and later
explicit imports retain the same published values and the initial
=TMPDIR=. The public format transformer returns 73 in both runs.
The Lean 4.34.1 replay independently compares the emitted order against
the native sequence, then checks the whole-image projection.

A negative control omits =std/net/address/serde~0= from that evaluation
step. A later import path still loads the same image and both receipts
match. It now publishes after =std/sync/rwlock~0=, at rank 215 instead
of 214. Compare ordered traces in addition to outputs and sets. The
ordinary socket API, socket types and datagram interfaces each contain
a direct import of the address facade.

**** Acceptance decision

The accepted result is the runtime evaluation schedule. Both arms still
read all 104 interfaces at Reader import and 111 at receipt. The
recursive cut is inadmissible with the current phase witness. Fourteen
positive-phase bodies remain outside the compiler's existing
lowered-phase classifier after resolving copied binaries to byte-matched
source. An unclassified body carries an unresolved obligation. The
operator census shows many generated macro objects use a local allocation
followed by writes to their own fields. A future phase checker needs
allocation provenance and phase-use evidence to distinguish those writes
from external registration, shared-state writes or earlier evaluation.
The ordinary path owns these visits. No new paired timing was run,
since the structural gate did not pass.

*** Scoped address expansion cut with a local phase ownership check

The active qualification goal holds =:std/io= and the Reader input,

=a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=.

The build-time checker reads the lowered Scheme paired with each actual

scopes. A write is allowed only when the receiver is such a structure
and the literal slot index is within its allocated fields. Immediate
calls outside the supported forms remain unknown. The controls reject
global writes, a shadowed object bound to an external value, and an
out-of-bounds field. Across the old 14 unclassified bodies, 13 now
qualify as local construction with recorded external reads and mark
origins. The five external constructor type descriptors already exist
before Reader startup in the isolated SDK. =std/encoding/hex~1= still
takes the ordinary visit. The selected interfaces have only the audited
declarations, literal loads and positive-phase imports.

The cut projects the three compiled socket callers that directly import
=:std/net/address= before recursive expansion. Positive-phase runtime
loads are read from the actual emitted =std/format/api= imports and
their native wrappers. They run in ordinary order. The runtime uses the
compiler-derived address schedule at the original initialization site.
A negative version that imported hex at phase zero loaded =hex~0=
before the address parser. The corrected version visits the hex
interface at expansion without changing the runtime order. Omitted
interface contexts can be materialized by later dynamic import.

The address-only trace identified 18 reopened interfaces under the

refer to format bindings owned by =std/format/ioutil=. The Writer
already imports that provider directly. The original compiled loader
closure for =std/format= is already resident after Reader in the
address candidate. The prototype verifies residency before projecting
the Writer's compiled interface. The Writer source keeps its original
imports.

Reader stays at 74 interface reads and 220 images. First receipt
improves structurally from 99 reads and 248 images in the address-only
candidate to 88 reads and 239 images. Lean verifies the two
ordered native inventories. Six native fixtures for JSON, numeric
JSON, Reader macros, reexports, format invocation, and format
reflection match ordinary. An additional timed cohort was not run.

timing gate still needs an improvement.

contains 46 phase-zero native bodies and another 22 native images.

and two lack byte-matched lowering. Treat the resulting screen as

**** Phase-zero native demand in the JSON Reader scope

A census of exact selected lowering covers 103 phase-zero bodies and
85 positive-phase bodies resident by the first receipt. Two artifacts
in each census lack byte-matched lowering and remain unresolved.
Record eager references independently from references under function
bodies. Five screened socket implementation bodies have eager incoming
method aliases in the compiled socket API. Their functions must be
available during registration. Six other phase-zero bodies are
independent of the scanned eager graph and have local definition
initializers. They form this experiment's deferred set.

The producer rewrites actual compiled consumers to obtain a delayed
binding through the current native loader and its original global
cell. Startup plan projections remove the six native activation
commands before recursive evaluation. The existing loader still owns
normal error reporting, artifact selection, and publication. The
isolation guard checks the selected paths and filesystem stamps before
selecting the native plan. Preparation also retains the compiled
artifact identity. This does not establish deployment-wide
immutability or complete provenance for the unclassified bodies.

The counterexamples establish three separate obligations.

- Changing a global loader binding did not change the native paths
  already bound to the original loader.
- Keeping the direct native symbol after a demand action emitted
  undefined-variable warnings at image link time. Replacing that
  reference with a read of the canonical cell after successful
  loading removed the warnings.
- A cached projected interface did not activate its runtime body on
  a subsequent computed import. The actual import evaluation now
  realizes requested bodies even when the expander context is ready.
  A separate failure showed that interface projections must be limited
  to the Reader and first-receipt consumer ancestry. An explicit format
  import later must evaluate its ordinary interface.

The successful first-demand fixture calls a native TCP function
without opening the old eager wrapper. Both socket bodies are absent
at the receipt and loaded by the first real TCP call. Both TCP calls
return 73. Their loader ordinals remain unchanged after repetition.
Computed import of the already registered socket interface realizes
its pending body and preserves its procedure identity on repeat. The
stale-artifact control changes native selection to an invalid object.
It admits no projections and fails at the same native image and with
the same load sequence as ordinary. Original macros, reexports,
numbers, format invocation and reflection, computed eval, and address
binary serde match the original semantic receipts. Lean verifies
the native projection at both boundaries and models reading the
original location only after successful native activation.

| Checkpoint | Prior interface reads | Candidate reads | Prior image count | Candidate image count |
|------------+-----------------------+-----------------+-------------------+-----------------------|
| Reader import | 74 | 72 | 220 | 214 |
| First receipt | 88 | 86 | 239 | 233 |

The unchanged ordinary reference reads 104 and 111 interfaces and
loads 244 and 254 objects. All six deferred bodies remain absent at
both measured boundaries. Every retained native image stays in
its previous relative order.

The paired run uses the original workload and watchdog with the
pinned staging and SDK inputs. It uses PR 1 head
=a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102= as the verified manual source
comparator. All 36 processes returned the same JSON and exited

|-----------------+-----------------+------------------+---------------+----------------------------------------+------------------------------------------|
| Reader import | 1466.91 | 1104.78 | 571.48 | -342.28 | +426.26 / +633.87 |
| First receipt | 1562.06 | 1306.21 | 927.29 | -237.73 | +264.91 / +501.41 |

**** Remaining import binding work after native demand

A diagnostic triplet instruments the same expander entry for ordinary,
native demand and manual. All three retain the expected JSON and
normal exit. At Reader import, actual import binding installations
number 97,213 ordinary, 70,872 candidate and 50,593 manual.
The candidate performs 77 read-or-plan calls, including the five
prepared interface selections. Actual interface reads remain 72.
The largest candidate-only binding owners are socket API (1,752),
server (1,646), socket (1,370), basic (1,304), stream (1,294),
delimited (1,261), and the two I/O facades (1,126 each).
At first receipt the next interval adds 14,617 binding installations
for the candidate and 29,084 for manual.

These counts identify remaining startup work and the affected
owners. The per-call instrumentation and one diagnostic triplet
do not establish wall-clock attribution. The next useful change
should avoid constructing unused imported bindings in these actual
compiled consumers. Preserve the original runtime activations and
the lexical contexts required by later macro invocations or
reflection. A prepared visibility view needs a way to realize its
original bindings when an opaque request falls outside the scope.

**** Keyed lookup for projected JSON Reader imports

The scanning prototype performed 4,939,269 export checks by Reader
import. The revised lookup indexes each source context once by exported
name and replays matching descriptors in their original order, including

**** Native initialization replay in JSON Reader

Reader retains =(import :std/io)=. The selected staging source is
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= with the existing SDK
=1cfb032=. The paired manual comparator remains PR #1 at
=a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=.

The producer verifies the standard runtime compiler output for each
prepared declaration. It accepts ignore forms, ordinary =__eval-import=
and 123 literal loader calls from 74 module bodies. Sixty loader
calls come from =std/io/socket/api=. Its checked literal contract
reduces to the same =__load-module= procedure with the same name.

These modules retain the ordinary atomic promise, phase visit schedule
and runtime import registry. The native promise executes the verified
commands directly. The body still retains the effective core syntax
with a simplified literal load expression. A replaced evaluator takes
the normal evaluation path. Metadata that would normally return void
after a final load still returns void. Lean checks ordered projection,
first-error behavior and the literal load contract.

The actual ordinary and compiled traces match all 140 native loads
and 95 ordered import groups through the same JSON receipt. The
malformed =parser~0.o1= fails with the same error content and native
prefix and caches the same exception on repeat. Raw backtraces differ
at the source-evaluator and native-promise frames and are retained.

All 74 observed contexts agree on binding names, macro aliases,
per-name ordered exports and normalized loader operands. The ten
pinned export sequences remain exact. The nine older semantic
fixtures, actual file reads and writes, TCP, dynamic imports, early
classes and scoped fallback also pass.

All 36 sampled processes returned the same receipt and exited normally.
The original 180 s watchdog and the forward and reverse cohorts apply.
The paired performance gate failed.
Total process CPU is -370 ms versus ordinary and +65 ms versus
manual by paired medians. In one same-engine diagnostic, exclusive
Reader initialization time outside recorded native loader calls
changes from 80 ms to 16 ms with the direct recipe. The socket API
module-body expansion sample changes from 45 ms to 6 ms.
The diagnostic estimates represent separate individual samples.

Reader still loads 43 more objects than manual. Fourteen of them
appear later in the manual trace by first receipt, primarily formatter
and serializer objects. The other 29 are absent from that manual
prefix. The object timing split identifies candidates for demand
analysis and preserves the original registration constraints.

**** Deferred numeric formatting bodies in JSON Reader

This acceptance run retains =(import :std/io)= with Git Cons staging
PR #1430 at =dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= and the frozen
=1cfb032= SDK with the selected reduced interfaces and native artifacts.
The staging Git tree records the Gambit gitlink =b3ff304f=. The live
manual PR #1 head was =a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=.

The lowered-code census joins binding ownership across ordinary bodies
and positive phase bodies. The candidate defers =std/number/writer~0=
and =std/format/env~integer-conversions~0=, and lowers each observed
cross-image use to load the original provider before reading its global
cell. The Reader socket declaration and the receipt's writer and format
environment both reach these providers. The receipt overlays pin both
interfaces and retain the ordinary fallback when an artifact is stale.

The first prototype drops two native images at Reader import. Ordinary
receipt imports activate them both. The second prototype also projects
those receipt declarations. Actual expansion of =(format "%d/%a" 73
'(ok))= then reports an undefined integer-conversion binding. The
positive image =std/format/api~1= was outside the startup census. The
candidate checks that phase edge from its byte-matched lowered source
and guards the actual use. The failure demonstrates that the
observed startup graph alone cannot justify pruning. Lean checks that
the concrete guard list is rejected without that phase edge.

=std/format/ioutil~0= stays eager in this experiment. The lowered JSON
Writer already references it. Rebuilding the float-formatting caller
first exposed a local GCC link failure under the selected Nix ld.
Only that overlay caller used the Apple linker and explicit SDK to
resolve =snprintf=; the workload, three reference arms and watchdog
were the same.

|----------+------------------------------+--------------------+----------------+--------------------|

The same two images stay absent at both checkpoints. The trace
changes from 238 to 234 events. Exactly four repeated literal loader
commands disappear. All other events, including ordered import groups,
remain in the same order. The arrival of the remaining required native
initializers is observed directly. A deliberately malformed writer
bundle rejects the projected plan, fails at the original activation
position and returns the same exception on repeat. Native TCP, early
datagram identity, dynamic eval, typed and ordinary macro invocation,
reexports, numeric JSON and temporary-directory capture pass. The Lean
prefix theorem is conditional on skipped loads having no visible
effect and succeeding; the compiled-plan stamp and corrupt-object
control provide concrete evidence for the tested guard.

**** Ordered first demand for the address parser branch

This continues the frozen JSON Reader and first JSON receipt scope. The
Reader retains =(import :std/io)=. Git Cons staging #1430 is pinned at
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= with Gambit gitlink =b3ff304f=.
The selected SDK remains =1cfb032= and the PR #1 manual comparator is
=a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=.

The compiled socket plan activates =std/text/pregexp~0= and
=std/net/address/parser~0= in sequence. The selected parser body
constructs two constant regexes using the first body and builds its
dispatch table from function bindings, including the already available
UnixAddress constructor. The source =pregexp= entry parameterizes its
space-sensitive parsing state for each pattern. The compiled startup
and receipt artifacts show no incoming use from those checkpoints.
The outside phase consumer =std/net/mime/struct~1= references
=pregexp-match=. Its presence among active consumers rejects the cut
in the concrete Lean check. Four byte-identified overlays still have
binary symbol checks only. This is a source and runtime witness for the
selected entry path, conditional on the completeness of that census.

The native loader's existing named registry still records the two
providers. The generated dependency order reaches pregexp then parser
on demand; the ordering check independently derives that schedule
from the lowered initializer reference. The original socket plan
still publishes address type and serialization registrations before
the Reader checkpoint. Directly importing address later produces
=inet4:127.0.0.1:73=, and reimport preserves both load-order indices
and the parser's hash-table identity.

Three runtime controls explain the admission rules. An early raw parser
activation before pregexp terminates with exit 139. An opaque,
already-built TCP consumer directly references the parser binding and
fails with an unbound operator when the barrier is disabled. Running
the barrier before a successful Reader import also produces warnings
in the native tempfile fixture because standard error bindings and
address types are missing. The final isolated harness arms the
barrier after the Reader import, and uses the existing module loader
to realize the dependency order before an opaque =load-module= call.
The same unchanged TCP object then exchanges byte 73 and repeats
without republishing the bodies. Arbitrary Gambit =load= paths and
concurrent production loader transitions still require source-level
qualification.

A malformed parser artifact fails the native stamp check. Against a
fresh, ordinary expansion with the same selected artifact paths, the
fallback preserves the exact ordered native prefix and rethrows the
same exception object on repetition. The earlier compiled-phase
configuration had a different prefix and is retained as a separate
control. Ordinary JSON, numeric, macro, rename/reexport, reflection,
typed macros, dynamic eval, native tempfile capture, and scoped
fallback checks pass. The recorded Reader/receipt activation trace
diff contains exactly the two removed literal load commands.

|----------+------------------------+-------------------------------+---------------------------------+----------------------|

| First receipt | 254 / 111 | 212 / 15 | 210 / 15 | 197 / 78 |

The original three arms retain their relative order in each of the

| First receipt | 993.92 | 689.43 | 682.45 | 661.17 | -6.98 | +27.18 / +22.77 |

The paired manual parity gate fails.

A separate, instrumented sample of the previous candidate registers
124 native load calls during Reader import with 242.20 ms inclusive,

**** Shared interface admission checks in JSON Reader

The workload retains =(import :std/io)= and uses the same pinned SDK,
staging source and PR #1 manual arm as the address parser run immediately
above. The candidate only changes validation of the already emitted
binding, positive-phase, reexport and declaration plans. It reuses
each resolved interface path within one validation pass, rechecks the
current stat fingerprint for every expected pin and discards the
observations before expansion or execution. A change to the search path
list clears the saved paths. The VM, resolved plans, object bytes and
active loading order are shared with the prior candidate.

Two diagnostic repetitions show 147 to 82 interface resolutions and
5,809 to 3,448 file-existence probes during admission, with the same
82 library identifiers. Reader and receipt traces remain identical to
the prior candidate, including 191 and 210 native objects and 3 and
15 interface reads. The original reference arms remain 244/254 native
objects and 104/111 interface reads for ordinary, and 152/197 native
objects and 53/78 interface reads for manual.

The live tests reject a wrong path, a wrong fingerprint and a real
replacement of a selected file at the same path. They also reselect
the provider on a search path change and independently resolve after
the validation pass. A separate real control places a new interface
in an existing higher-priority root between two validations while
keeping the search path list unchanged. The ordinary resolver then
selects the new interface and the prototype still accepts the old
path. Lean proves equivalence conditional on stable resolution and
exhibits the same counterexample. This prototype is rejected for
integration. It has no semantic proof for concurrently populated roots.

The frozen-layout semantic tests include macro and reexport, computed
eval, native address and TCP demands, repeat identity, numeric JSON,
early tempfile state and stale-parser fallback. The first-error
prefix and the cached exception match the ordinary control.

| Boundary | Ordinary median ms | Prior median ms | Shared median ms | Manual median ms | Shared minus prior paired ms | Shared minus manual forward / reverse paired ms |
|----------+--------------------+-----------------+------------------+------------------+-----------------------------+-------------------------------------------------|
| Reader import | 2098.37 | 1171.01 | 1165.62 | 834.95 | +85.40 | +236.24 / +333.75 |
| First receipt | 2210.58 | 1535.17 | 1432.56 | 1264.42 | +95.48 | -50.72 / +149.23 |

All 48 processes exit naturally with the expected receipt under the
original 180-second watchdog. Timings apply to the frozen path layout.
The same-run comparisons have wide dispersion. The candidate's paired
difference against the prior version ranges across zero at both
boundaries. The unchanged manual arm's full-process median is 2.725 s
in this run and 1.395 s in the address parser run. No speedup or
reproducible wall-clock regression is established by this diagnostic.
The positive Reader paired margins fail the manual gate. The previous
candidate remains the selected prototype.

Independent instrumented runs report 122 Reader native loader calls
with 83 distinct images and at most 0.05 ms inclusive across the repeat
calls. First activations consume 252 to 323 ms inclusive. The Reader
has 39 image identities absent from the manual arm. Thirteen are in
=std/io= and sum to 38 to 64 ms of the recorded first activations.
Five socket bodies in that group have 20 incoming eager lowered uses
from the method aliases published by =socket/api=.
Those bodies need a publication-preserving transformation before the
compiler can remove their eager activation. Counting the first
physical load and required initialization is more informative than
counting duplicate calls into the loaded-module registry. Inclusive
debugger timings are individual observations and include profiler cost.

selected =1cfb032= SDK, manual PR #1 =a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=,
and Reader import =(import :std/io)=. The prior comparison arm is the
address parser branch candidate.

The emitter reads the selected byte-identified lowered native inputs
for =basic=, =client=, =stream=, =server= and =datagram=. It requires a
unique, owned definition for each binding and accepts closures and
compiler timestamps. It preserves the original binding symbols and
emits the 49 definitions into one native object. The admitted declaration
plan loads it at the fifth original activation, after the prerequisite
imports and before the API publishes its 20 method aliases. The exact
ordered trace removes four literal activations and changes the fifth
operand. The rest of the recorded import and initialization events match
the prior candidate. Lean checks order and the actual binding set,
including rejection when the carrier follows the API.

The regular runtime lookup for an original body name uses the resident
provider after coalescing. Calling it without this redirection reproduced
republishing and unequal procedure values. The final probe sees each
method equal to the defining procedure and all 49 values stable after
computed eval imports and =load-module=. A preloaded original body
rejects the grouped plan before it can replace that value. A malformed
original client object rejects the plan and fails at the same position
with the same cached exception as ordinary. Stale phase, reexport and
declaration plans use all five independent bodies. An earlier version
also placed the group in fallback forms. The archived early fallback
observations explain why the group is now emitted only into admitted
compiled declarations.

The actual =(udp-socket address)= expansion exposed a missing
=InetAddress= syntax binding in the prior and first grouped candidates.
The existing delayed binding publication now checks its pinned
direct provider =:std/net/address/types= when a macro needs it. The
ordinary comparator and repaired candidate both exchange the exact
three UDP bytes using the macro. This covers sendto/recvfrom and connected
send/recv at fixed loopback endpoints. The preceding address-discovery
probe encountered a Darwin address-conversion error in ordinary too and
is retained separately. The unchanged native TCP input also passes. The independent type-only
variant uses the prior artifacts with all five bodies loaded
separately. It produces the same UDP bytes and matches the prior
native activation trace byte for byte. This verifies the type fix
independently of native object grouping.
JSON, numeric, macro, reexport, native tempfile and address identity
checks pass in their selected scopes.

The direct Gambit =(load <old-basic-object-path>)= is a counterexample.
Ordinary rejects the duplicate physical file, while the grouped
prototype opens the old image and changes the procedure value. That
entry bypasses the Gerbil named-loader redirection. The runtime
prototype is rejected for integration even if timings improve.

**** Native linker identities for grouped socket publication

The fixed Reader retains =(import :std/io)=. The exact staging source
is =dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= with Gambit gitlink
=b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228=. The comparison runs on the
same selected =1cfb032= SDK and manual PR #1
=a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=. The matched prior candidate
is the address parser branch.

The prior single-module carrier erased the Gambit linker identities
for five bodies. A direct =(load <basic~0.o1>)= could then load the old
physical body and change the procedure identity. The Gambit source
shows that the native duplicate error is raised after the selected
linker returns a module or linkfile descriptor whose version indicates
prior setup. This check is below Gerbil's named-loader registry.

The compiler now retains each lowered member's generated native
module and joins their descriptors through the existing GSC flat
linker. The installed carrier and five member paths share one inode.
Each path exposes its regular Gambit entry. Before grouped
activation, a member entry initializes only that member. After the
group has initialized, each member entry returns the marked group
descriptor and Gambit raises its ordinary duplicate exception. The
raw caller of any of the five installed paths gets the same code
=-507510773= as both the independent entry control and the original SDK arm, with the defining procedure
unchanged. The member-first group entry rejects an already initialized
member. The Gerbil admission scan also checks the lowered body
markers, including bodies initialized through raw Gambit load outside
the Gerbil registry. This is a real runtime fallback, with no reset of
the Gambit descriptor state.

All 20 method aliases still refer to their canonical procedures.
All 49 named values stay identical after repeated computed imports
and named native entry. The observed initialization trace is the same
as the preceding grouping experiment. A malformed member fails at
the original position and repeated imports rethrow the same exception
object. Wrong phase, reexport and declaration plans use independent
member entry points and do not choose the carrier entry. Native TCP,
typed UDP, JSON and ordinary semantic controls pass. Lean checks
the earlier schedule, method readiness, direct duplicate behavior
and member-first rejection. These are sequential validations of
the selected artifact layout. Concurrent native loading and
alternate absolute paths into another SDK have no qualification here.
The candidate remains an isolated compiler and loader prototype.

The fixed Reader retains =(import :std/io)=. The exact staging source is

=b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228=. The benchmark runs on the same

the five-member native socket link group, which retains its earlier
duplicate-load behavior in this run.

The =tempfile= initializer captures =TMPDIR= during the Reader import.
The crypto wrapper also introduces a foreign =libcrypto.3.dylib=
dependency. This experiment retains both initializations at their
original points. It uses 20 byte-identified, source-matched native
members from I/O, format, serialization and OS file support. The
native inputs and rebuilt images have only the system-library
dependency and no Mach-O initializer section. The two =file~0=
members use separate bundles since they require the same link symbol.

The two bundles share physical storage across the original filenames.
The exported Gambit linker for each filename returns that member's
original, separately compiled descriptor. Every Scheme initializer
still runs at its original position. Admission rechecks the original
image pin, the selected alias pin and the unbound body marker before
adding the alternate load root. A stale alias pin keeps the original
roots. A damaged original counter object keeps the original failure
prefix and returns the same exception on repeat. Direct raw duplicate
loads for all 20 active aliases match the original error codes and
leave 20 representative procedures unchanged. The five socket entry
controls, TCP, typed UDP, macro expansion, reexports, dynamic eval and
ordinary comparison fixtures pass.

A first native rebuild passed loading with warnings because the
format reader's Gambit sentinels were treated as undefined globals.
An interactive =-e= include did not supply their compile-time context.
The explicit Gambit compiler prelude restored macro expansion.
Both failed traces are retained. The final logical native and import
event trace matches the previous candidate byte for byte. Lean
checks the invariant that changing only image placement preserves
entry order and checks the concrete observed traces. Concurrent raw
loading and other SDK layouts are outside these observed checks.

**** General plan and native image placement

The frozen JSON Reader performance reference is the measured
=462.59 ms= import median and =725.57 ms= first receipt median,
with =(import :std/io)=. The same 48 observations compare the original,
manual PR #1, and two automatic candidates under the 180-second
watchdog. The paired evidence in the previous section remains the
acceptance reference for this workload. The summary has SHA-256
=4b748c41c1c1243bbc8e6cb445921cce9c4616a0bc57aca27f67a327dc54e718=.

The compiler resolves a reference to its defining module, phase and
binding slot. The worklist closes those references and the required
initializers before the loader uses the resulting ordered plan.
Macros can produce references to private bindings. A reexport
preserves the defining identity. Dynamic and opaque observations
preserve the ordinary evaluation obligation. Module activation and
failure publication remain in the existing module registry.

Physical image placement is a separate compiler and linker decision.
Modules that share an image still have separate native entries. An
image and linker pair must identify a single member. The activation
trace retains each logical module in its original position. Count
each newly mapped image once against the process's own starting
snapshot. The cost comparison includes plan selection and validation.
Native file placement may improve startup even when the logical
activation graph stays the same.

The existing prototype admits the packed paths into the process
search path. A controlled replacement of one alternate object
succeeds with ordinary fallback when it precedes admission. The same
replacement after admission leaves the stale path selected and exits
70 in the native loader. Two members sharing one image otherwise
preserved their individual procedure identities under 16 concurrent
first-load calls. That fixture has two distinct logical members and
compares the ordinary and packed paths.

The integration must bind the native entry to the artifact actually
opened. An already opened immutable artifact can finish publishing
while a new installation becomes available. If the observed image
at open differs from the prepared image, the loader uses the ordinary
provider from the selected logical generation. This rule requires
a stable artifact from open through publication. A path or a stat
performed earlier cannot establish it. In-place path substitution is
an experimental counterexample to the current prototype. The model
assumes an atomic open with stable identity, and the actual build and
loader must demonstrate that property.

Lean proves for arbitrary member, image and linker types that
unambiguous entries resolve to their defining member, opened
generations must match, and changing placement preserves logical
activation order. It defines the exact newly mapped image set and
charges each image once. Replay reads the exported linker symbols from
all six actual Mach-O files, checks the 75 entry routes, and replays
the recorded mapping sets. Import replays 115 to 64 newly mapped
images and first receipt 134 to 83. The ordinary and candidate
logical activations agree at 187 and 206. Each runtime trace uses
its own 56-image pre-import snapshot.

Quint models two members, two workers and two installed generations.
Its six executable cases cover stable sharing, replacement on either
side of open, conflicting linker names and the two rejected
implementations. The bounded safety claim covers publication order,
ownership and artifact identity. Settlement assumes a fair enabled
publication. TLC generated 170 states and exhausted 65 distinct
reachable states with zero left in the queue. The safety and temporal
checks passed. Both negative controls produced counterexamples.
The shared dispatcher deferred because its task dependencies were
occupied. The retained TLC logs come from a local run of the same
registered model. The contract assumes stable opened artifacts.

The compiler change records a resolved provider while generating
runtime or phase code. The initial source patch captures references
and assignments, retaining an explicit absent owner for backend
symbols without a resolved source binding. Both changed modules pass
MCP balance and checkout-local C-target expansion. The normal
=gxc -S= path from the selected SDK emits both changed compiler
artifacts. The native =stage1= build and use of the emitted data by
the standard loader are separate pending checks. This scope keeps
the JSON Reader and its first receipt as the native performance
workload.

**** Reader native images in a fixed installed generation

The ordinary loader can use separate entry names in an installed
shared image. This check uses the same Homebrew =1cfb032= toolchain and
the staging and Gambit pins recorded above. The fixed Reader source
still imports =:std/io=.

The two-generation control copied the SDK library and installed one
14-member image at its conventional module paths in the old
generation. The new generation retains its original separate images.
Neither library directory was edited while the process was running.
Under the standard =load-module=, an implicit root reached via
=current= selects the old generation for the first load and the new
generation after publication. Capturing the real SDK directory before
startup keeps both loads in the old generation. A fresh process after
publication resolves both modules in the new generation. All cases
exit zero and preserve repeated procedure identity within the process.

The complete Reader replay installs the same 75 entry files in six
images and selects the actual directory at launch on the existing
library search path. It retains the experimental expansion plan.
Its load path stays unchanged after launch. Native artifact pins are
regenerated for the relocated images and unrelated pin rows are
matched exactly. The original and relocated image bytes are checked.
The relative alias layout retains a single image per group.

| Checkpoint | Actual .ssi reads | Newly mapped native files | Logical activations |
|------------+-------------------+---------------------------+---------------------|
| Reader import | 3 | 64 | 187 |
| First JSON receipt | 15 | 83 | 206 |

Interface reads, native mapping order after path normalization,
and logical activation order match the measured preceding candidate.
The receipt is =[false,null,[true]]= with natural exit zero. Hygiene,
reexports, dynamic eval, socket identity, early TMPDIR capture, and
first native TCP demand retain their previous observations. The
corrupt higher-priority provider reproduces the identical failure
prefix and repeated exception identity. Both fixed-endpoint UDP
runs match the earlier =[73,19,41]= receipt. The typed dynamic endpoint
variant fails with =No route to host= in the ordinary arm, the
previous candidate and the installed candidate. The peer-derived
destination variant also fails in both candidates. Both UDP
failures remain in the record.

Two source patches target the standard runtime and expander. The
implicit SDK library path is normalized during runtime initialization,
while explicit library search paths retain their existing behavior.
The expander consults a prepared compiler input before opening the
interface. A missing or malformed input uses the ordinary reader; a
reload always reads the file. Both patches pass MCP balance,
checkout-local C-target expansion and =gxc -S=. The actual lowered
input helper has a native check that observes zero ordinary reads
for the admitted input and one for each of the fallback cases.

The native build still needs to combine the compiler's binding
producer, the expander input and the installed artifacts in one
standard executable. The install replay covers atomic publication
of distinct library generations. Mutation inside an already selected
generation retains the earlier failure counterexample. The fixed
performance comparison remains the original paired workload.

**** Native compiler producer and staging address inputs

The standard =stage1= target built Gerbil
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62= with the compiler reference
observer on Gambit =b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228=.
The linked executable's observed version agrees with both pins.
With the standard compiler API, the test provider emits a phase-one
helper reference. The consumer emits a reference to the defining
provider through a renamed surface and a private binding introduced
by a hygienic transformer. The compiler emits both runtime and
phase Scheme artifacts. Each standalone fixture exits zero.

A direct Reader compilation using Homebrew =1cfb032= native phase
objects rejects =std/error~1.o1= for a different C compiler or
options. A source-only diagnostic on the same Reader reaches the
180-second watchdog before producing binding rows. Both attempts
are retained. The admitted performance reference continues to use
the earlier matched Homebrew workload.

The standard staging stdlib compiler produced the recorded Reader
loader with =:std/io= intact. Traversing explicit lowered
=load-module= commands from Reader and the full JSON entry gives
224 logical loader and image names across 117 emitted interfaces.
The interfaces declare 105 positive-phase visits. These are
compiler declarations, with ordinary runtime observations still
required for actual loads and effects.

Three emitted socket loaders point directly to =std/net/address=.
They are =socket/api=, =socket/types= and =socket/datagram=.
The generator derives these callers from the fresh compiled graph
and records their actual compiled interface inputs. It derives 63
ordered Address runtime bodies from the lowered wrappers. The two
explicit phase-one imports of the actual format API close over
three native bodies in order. The generic Lean worklist checks the
two emitted schedules against the independently read compiler graph.
Removing a required native row rejects the plan.

The earlier 14 phase inputs were copied from the fresh compiler
output and hashed. The existing checker revalidates 13 local
constructor bodies. The remaining Hex visit is retained in its
ordinary phase. Lean verifies all 32 writes into its 103-byte vector
and rejects the 102-byte bound on the fresh lowered evidence.
These checks classify the emitted forms. They preserve the earlier
runtime obligations and require the matching native objects for
the integrated replay.

The combined source patch adds transformer invocation records and
the emitted runtime and phase artifacts to the optional compiler
dependency observer. The compiler collects transformer events while
the driver lock is held and delivers them after release. The same
source patch includes SDK root normalization and the prepared input
entry before the ordinary interface reader. Balance and targeted
C-context expansion passed. In the incremental attempt, source
relative lowering reached the watchdog and the compiled-interface
overlay lacked the expected source prelude. The normal =stage1=
target is building the combined source. The standard library build
also continues to produce matching native artifacts.

**** Frozen Reader reference and a general checkpoint contract

The same automatic JSON Reader candidate with =(import :std/io)=
is the frozen performance reference at both boundaries. Import
was =462.59 ms= and time from startup to =[false,null,[true]]=
was =725.57 ms=. All 48 observations from the four arms remain
available. Both timing files are pinned by SHA-256. The replay
recomputed the medians and the paired differences from the raw
records. The original summary's =forward= and =reverse= labels
refer to blocks 1–6 and 7–12. Both blocks interleave the relative
execution order. Grouping the same samples by actual order gives
these median candidate-minus-manual differences.

| Boundary | Candidate before manual | Manual before candidate |
|----------+-------------------------+-------------------------|
| Import | -32.34 ms | -283.73 ms |
| First receipt | -166.68 ms | -502.13 ms |

Each cell has six pairs. The largest candidate regressions against
manual remain +990.02 ms for import and +1221.59 ms for first
receipt. These are observations from the isolated Homebrew-based
candidate. The staging compiler and standard SDK still require
the unchanged native parity test.

The design separates the compiler's ordered logical obligations
from physical placement. A later request can publish a new logical
member of a resident image. It runs that member's initialization
at the original point and carries the same identity. The earlier
checkpoint's mapped set and the process's initial snapshot both
exclude images from the later charge. Compare cumulative time at
each boundary using its own bound, including admission overhead.
A receipt improvement cannot offset an import regression. Even
zero new mappings can lose on warm-path overhead. Cost bounds
require separate measurements.

Lean proves for arbitrary member and image types that the two
new-mapping sets partition the combined mapping set exactly.
The frozen native replay reproduces the candidate's observed 64
new images at import and 83 by receipt, with 19 added between
those boundaries. The prior placement mapped 115 and 134. The
Quint model adds a third logical member requested after import,
a prewarmed image, and replacement between checkpoints. Ten
executable cases passed. TLC exhausted 216 distinct reachable
states with none left in the queue and checked fair claim
settlement. TLC rejects stale opens, ambiguous entry ownership,
and charging the shared image twice. The model treats the image
observed at open as immutable through publication.

The same-ABI staging compiler separately emitted the real Reader
reference log with input hashes unchanged. A native recursive
import fixture read the provider interface once on its ordinary
path, zero times from a valid prepared row, and once for an
invalid row. All three returned 73. This fixture qualifies the
admission branch on the selected ABI. It does not qualify the
whole Reader projection or standard SDK integration. The next
native scope keeps the Reader workload and varies the starting
residency and the installation generation. The real loader must
preserve initialization, macro and dynamic evaluation semantics
while producing the expected mapping deltas.

**** Composing the Reader plan across scopes

The Homebrew-based six-image candidate remains the fixed Reader
reference. The source retains =(import :std/io)= and the first
receipt is =[false,null,[true]]=. The inputs are staging
=dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=, its Gambit gitlink
=b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228=, Homebrew SDK =1cfb032=,
and the manual PR #1 at =a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102=.
Rechecking all 48 timed observations against their archived hashes
reproduces both medians and all four order-specific paired results.
Each order has six pairs. The watchdog remains 180 seconds.

| Boundary | Candidate median | Prior → candidate new images | Candidate − manual, candidate first | Candidate − manual, manual first |
|----------+------------------+------------------------------+-------------------------------------+----------------------------------|
| Reader import | 462.59 ms | 115 → 64 | −32.34 ms | −283.73 ms |
| First receipt | 725.57 ms | 134 → 83 | −166.68 ms | −502.13 ms |

The logical activation counts are 187 and 206 in both automatic
placements. Individual regressions against the manual arm reach
+990.02 ms at import and +1221.59 ms at receipt. The separate
six-bundle staging cohort missed all four paired latency gates.
Use the frozen run as a placement and benchmark reference, and use
ordinary execution as the oracle for semantics.

Lean proves that validated scopes on the same exact compiler graph
compose by union of required obligations. Keep phase-qualified
bindings, transformers, and observable initializers in that graph.
Their execution still follows the ordinary schedule. An unresolved
or opaque obligation takes the existing ordinary path. Placement
runs after the logical plan and may reuse a physical image for
distinct ordered linker entries. For any number of checkpoints,
Lean proves that the newly mapped image sets are disjoint and that
their union equals the complete run's new image set. The executable
Reader replay attributes 64 images to import and 19 additional
images to the first receipt. A modeled overlapping request then
adds zero images. Every cumulative latency bound must pass on its
own, including admission work.

Quint covers three checkpoints, four logical members, two workers,
and installation replacement. Its second scope can activate its
own member in an image already mapped by the first receipt. If the
installation changes before open, the same demand uses the ordinary
image and is charged for that new mapping. The control that resets
residency at a scope boundary charges the receipt image again and is
rejected. All 13 executable cases pass. TLC exhausts 490 distinct
reachable states with zero pending, checks fair claim settlement,
and reproduces all four registered negative controls. The shared
DEV dispatcher deferred because the task dependencies were occupied.
The retained TLC logs use the same model bytes as the verifier
registration.

A separate staging differential isolates the largest candidate-only
import span in the phase profile. The span for =std/io/socket/api.ssi=
was 402.13 ms in one instrumented candidate process. The typed socket
declaration replay preserves binding and export observations, the
ordered native trace, the receipt, the hygienic transformer result,
and the dynamic address import. Both paths read 74 interfaces at
import and 99 by the receipt. This experiment has no paired latency
result and gives no evidence of fewer loads. Keep the subsequent
native experiment on the Reader's actual import and dynamic-demand
sequence. Compare opened artifacts and activation identity through
the existing loader under unchanged watchdog and receipt conditions.
Broader package qualification remains open.

**** Same-ABI staging Reader projection and a provider counterexample

The pinned staging Gerbil =dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=
and Gambit =b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228= ran the
three generated socket interface rows in a standalone Reader
process. Both arms used the same native admission object and the
same standard library providers. The candidate selected the rows
before the recursive interface read. The input digests for the
earlier compiler graph matched before and after both runs.
JSON Reader retains its =:std/io= import.

| Checkpoint | Ordinary .ssi reads | Prepared .ssi reads | Ordinary new logical native entries | Prepared new logical native entries |
|------------+---------------------+---------------------+-------------------------------------+-------------------------------------|
| Reader import | 104 | 74 | 190 | 166 |
| First JSON receipt | 111 | 99 | 200 | 194 |
| After explicit dynamic eval | 111 | 108 | 200 | 200 |

Both arms returned =[false,null,[true]]= and exited with code zero
within the 180-second watchdog. The candidate skipped the
=std/net/address.ssi= read until explicit dynamic import and kept
all three socket rows scoped to the Reader. Datagram publication
occurred before the import checkpoint in both arms. Repeated
import preserved the class and the tempfile parameter identities,
and the parameter captured the earlier =TMPDIR=. The format
transformer returned 73. A dynamic address round trip also
verified the procedure identity across the address reexport and
the defining API. Both arms rejected the initial fixture's
unqualified address literal with the same contract error. The
corrected fixture uses the canonical =ip4:127.0.0.1= input.

Every observed =~0= completion stays in the same relative order.
The full sequence differs. The source pass classifies 63
named positive-phase constructor images that are delayed or
cross a completion, and retains the ordinary hex phase visit.
Lean replays the preserved completion order at all four
checkpoints after projecting only those 63 images. The checker
rejects moving or dropping a retained completion. The lowering
audit identifies ordinary hygiene-context imports as separate
obligations. Completion order and constructor classification
do not establish that every such operation is admissible in a
different installation generation.

An isolated higher-priority corrupt =std/net/address/parser~1.o1=
exposes this boundary. Ordinary import exits 70 before the
Reader import checkpoint. Without a provider check, the candidate
emits the JSON receipt and fails only at the later explicit
dynamic import. A diagnostic provider check detects the changed
selection, uses the ordinary path and fails before Reader import.
The guarded and ordinary arms have the same interface prefix
and report the same native failure. This check handles a changed
provider path in this fixture. In-place replacement after
selection still requires an immutable installation witness or
an equivalent existing loader guarantee.

The exact five comparator source blobs from Gerbil PR #1
=a0eaef4412f48613b639b9586e854b971b30c102= were verified against
GitHub and against the locally retained source. The five-edit
patch applies cleanly to staging. The native comparator build is
separate from this semantic replay. The full unmodified stdlib
build was stopped by Clang receiving SIGKILL on the generated
Djot phase image. The Reader closure itself is available and was
used here. No new timing claim is made from these instrumented
runs. The standard compiler/SDK integration and the same-load
performance parity test remain open.

**** Same-path provider changes before and after Reader admission

The native Reader fixture selects a private copy of the staging
=std/net/address/parser~1.o1= artifact. All cases use the actual
same-ABI expander hook and preserve =(import :std/io)=. In the
mutable cases, the writer changes the first four bytes in place.
The path, device, inode, size and mtime match their original
values. The fixture checks both provider bytes and the selected
policy at admission.

| Control | Change point | Policy accepts | First receipt | Exit |
|---------+--------------+----------------+---------------+------|
| Path check | Before admission | yes | yes | 70 |
| File stamp check | Before admission | yes | yes | 70 |
| Byte comparison | Before admission | no | no | 70 |
| Byte comparison | At the first prepared row | yes | yes | 70 |
| Immutable artifact | At the first prepared row, both writes denied | yes | yes | 0 |

The ordinary failure occurs before Reader import completes.
A byte mismatch before admission selects that same ordinary
failure. Changing the bytes after admission delays the failure
until explicit dynamic =eval= even with a matching earlier byte
comparison. Darwin's user-immutable flag denied both in-place
writing and replacement of the private file at its existing path.
The Reader returned the expected JSON, preserved its observations
and exited zero. The fixture restores the flag after each run.

The general plan compares all deferred provider identities for
the selected installation and holds the selected generation
stable through the last deferred lookup. A later publication may
select a new generation for future processes. The current
staging installer copies files into the configured version
prefix. It can update an existing generation in place, so the
normal install path still needs a stable publication contract
before enabling deferral by default. An earlier successful
snapshot check cannot supply that contract.

Lean proves that the installed identity list remains the
expected one under the retained-generation premise. The
separate Quint model compares failure boundaries for ordinary
and deferred import. Four scenarios pass. TLC exhausts 11
reachable states, with zero left in the queue, and finds both
the same-stamp and post-check mutation counterexamples. The
model's sealed state assumes stable contents through demand.
The native immutable result covers the tested object.

All five hand-narrowed modules from the exact PR #1 source
blobs now compile on the staging ABI. The resource collector
in the first timing attempt ran inside the sandbox. Reader
printed both timings, the expected JSON and OK in 176 seconds,
and the outer =time -l= exited 1 because =kern.clockrate= was
unavailable. This attempt stays in the evidence. The same
three-arm workload and 180-second watchdog run with the
working resource collector on the retained sealed input copies.

| First receipt | 9422.29 | 8936.37 | 8433.33 | +455.63 | +962.17 | +1130.40 |

| First receipt | 194 / 143 | 29 / 22 | 99 / 78 |

Manual has no unique logical object or interface at either

The staging build inputs support the six earlier native groups.

The combined staging source completed normal =make stage1=.
Its first launch failed in runtime initialization because
=path-normalize= was applied to the configured SDK library
directory before that directory existed. An explicit existing
Gambit root let the newly compiled executable exercise the
ordinary compiler and expander directly. Compilation of the
unchanged JSON Reader produced 2,238 reference events, 15,727
transformer events across the expansion context, one interface,
one runtime body and one emitted positive-phase body. Both
recursive import arms returned the expected JSON, preserved
the early class, parameter and macro observations, and exited
zero. They reproduced the earlier logical load counts using
the combined compiler's own admission hook. The diagnostic
global read override was bypassed by compiled calls, leaving
the interface-read metric unavailable in this particular run.

The initialization source now normalizes an installed library
path when that path exists, preserving build-tree startup
before installation. The change passed the existing Gerbil MCP
balance and syntax checks. Targeted native compilation and
the ordinary Bach relink are the remaining startup checks.

The source inputs contain 119,865 bytes. The table contains

JSON Reader keeps =(import :std/io)= and the three original
Address projection rows. The pre-expansion hook supplies the
decoded input for the additional interfaces only below the
Reader root. The expander still builds the module contexts and

=[false,null,[true]]=, publish the same early class, preserve
the =TMPDIR= parameter and produce 73 through the format macro.
Out-of-scope dynamic address import still reads the ordinary
interface. The complete ordered logical native traces match at

|----------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------+------------------|

| First receipt | 99 | 78 | 78 |

The resulting interface sets equal the manual sets. Their
