Eight alternating in-process pairs isolate reading and decoding

costs. The table's isolated decode result does not establish

The targeted compilation and normal Bach relink finished with
exit 0. =run.sh gxi --version= reports Gerbil =dbdf763= and
Gambit =b3ff304= before the configured installation directory
exists. This invocation uses the normal build wrapper, without
the explicit Gambit-root argument used to diagnose the earlier
failure.

The ordinary and prepared Reader cases use this executable's
compiled pre-expansion hook directly. The probe observes each
fallback at that hook, immediately before the ordinary reader
runs. The two checkpoints report 104 to 74 and 111 to 99
interface reads. Address is absent from the prepared reads
until the explicit dynamic import. Both processes return the
expected JSON, preserve the early class, temporary directory
parameter and macro observations, and exit 0.

The isolated corrupt parser provider still fails at Reader
import in the ordinary case. The unguarded projection lets
the first receipt escape before the later dynamic failure.
The scoped path check sees the changed provider, takes the
ordinary path and reproduces the import failure and exact
interface prefix. The same-path mutation and retention
conditions remain covered separately.

The first six-bundle timing launch had a harness variable

manual library tree and exited before reaching Reader. The
failed invocation is retained. The corrected harness checks
the test script's path before locking any input and uses the

**** Six-bundle Reader paired timing and native file footprint

The timed candidate combines the three Address projection rows
and six native bundles on the retained staging ABI. Reader
keeps =(import :std/io)=. The common fixed SDK, PR #1 manual
artifacts, workload and 180-second watchdog match the earlier
cohort. This timing uses the prepared Address inputs from that
cohort. The 21 additional interface values are tested separately.

All 36 launches returned =[false,null,[true]]= and exited zero.
The longest complete process took 60.68 seconds. Each
candidate/manual order has six pairs. The comparison recomputes
all four paired medians from the raw process records.

| Boundary | Ordinary median ms | Candidate median ms | Manual median ms | Candidate minus manual, candidate first ms | Candidate minus manual, manual first ms |
|----------+--------------------+---------------------+------------------+--------------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------|
| Reader import | 14349.73 | 20928.16 | 13066.00 | +3829.50 | +4827.32 |
| First receipt | 15230.73 | 25881.63 | 18183.06 | +3793.62 | +4100.64 |

The candidate misses the parity threshold in at least one execution order.

Median total wall time minus user and system CPU time was
14.25 s for ordinary, 30.70 s for candidate and
21.69 s for manual. This is a process-level accounting
lower bound on time outside CPU execution. It covers startup,
the two workload boundaries and exit. It does not locate the
wait or establish its cause. Module expansion and initialization
timing run separately from the benchmark.

The dyld snapshots also support a file-size comparison, distinct
from resident memory or actual bytes read.

| Boundary | Packed candidate mappings / summed file bytes | Manual mappings / summed file bytes |
|----------+----------------------------------------------+-------------------------------------|
| Reader import | 98 / 35,111,328 | 98 / 29,098,024 |
| First receipt | 126 / 46,286,336 | 143 / 45,363,560 |

At import the largest extra file is =libcrypto.3.dylib= at
4,874,336 bytes. The selected native =std/crypto/libcrypto~0=
provider links it, and the actual dylib declares =__init_offsets=.
This is an opaque native initializer boundary for the present
pruning analysis. Reader's direct source bindings provide no
basis for dropping that initializer. Candidate-only positive
phase files add 3,344,152 bytes. The six bundled images still
reduce file bytes compared with those members' separate images.

**** Reader native lookup cost and socket expression ownership

The nine actual uses of =std/io/interface= expose the same 503
ordered source exports. A direct lookup uses the current context
table for a plain phase-zero key and falls back to ordinary
resolution on a miss, marked key or other phase. Both
lookup implementations are optimized native code in this test.
They produce the identical binding objects for all 503 exports.
A temporary provider rebinding remains visible to both paths.
Lean proves equality against the ordinary first-table lookup and
preserves phase-sensitive fallback.

Eight warmed pairs each resolve the actual exports 251,500 times.
Median lookup times are 2.065 and 1.067 microseconds per operation.
The median paired saving is 1.018 microseconds. Applied linearly
to the 4,024 repeated positions of this provider, it illustrates
about 4.10 ms. This isolates lookup. It does not time descriptor
allocation, binding insertion or complete Reader execution.
The first comparison used an interpreted candidate and a native
baseline. Its retained trace cannot determine native lookup cost.

Separate typed and manual Reader profiles exercise the same
receipt. In one sample per arm, Reader import observes 1,759 and
1,385 import spans and 1,357 and 1,066 compile spans. The interval
after import through first receipt has the same operation counts
in both arms. The largest candidate-only import site remains
=std/io/socket/api.ssi=. A further typed replay measures its own
declaration actions after subtracting nested instrumented spans.

| Socket API operation | Occurrences | Time outside nested timed actions |
|----------------------+-------------+-----------------------------------|
| Import | 2 | 10.85 ms |
| Phase visit | 2 | 12.30 ms |
| Runtime declaration | 72 | 1.84 ms |
| Syntax declaration | 8 | 1.76 ms |
| Second-pass expressions | 64 | 335.02 ms |
| Export | 1 | 0.21 ms |

The socket profile identifies the second-pass expressions as
the dominant measured work in this scope. It preserves the
24 prepared inputs, early temporary directory capture, normal
exit and original receipt. This instrumentation records a single
execution per arm. The uninstrumented parity cohort remains the
latency acceptance criterion.

**** Bound the remaining whole-import work and preserve descriptor identity

The prepared phase-zero program still contains 308 whole imports.
Counting export rows from the 74 previously observed module contexts
covers 283 of those edges and projects 41,316 descriptor constructions
if each covered edge converts the full observed export list. The
remaining 25 edges have providers outside this context snapshot.
The counts describe the snapshot and the ordinary construction rule.
They do not measure constructor time.

| Source | Covered whole imports | Export rows per import | Projected descriptors |
|--------+-----------------------+------------------------+-----------------------|
| =std/io/interface= | 22 | 503 | 11,066 |
| =std/error= | 44 | 210 | 9,240 |

The two providers account for 49.1% of the projected descriptors.
The existing whole-import step converts these provider rows again
for each consumer. The prepared syntax replay leaves that step in place.

Gerbil exports mutators for the =module-import= records and the list
stored in each =import-set=. Two ordinary native consumers of
=std/io/interface= receive independently mutable =Socket= descriptors.
A control that reuses the import list makes a rename in the first
consumer observable in the second. Lean checks both the independent
consumer cells and the shared-cell counterexample. A future prepared
representation must preserve that per-consumer identity and keep any
shared compiler metadata behind the existing mutable objects.

The exact generated =runtime.scm= from the failed native build also
compiles with the corrected SDKROOT using the same Gambit compiler.
It produces an arm64 Mach-O bundle. The complete stage1 build is a
separate gate, and the native checks wait for its successful exit.

that admission. It reproduces the six-image gap to the frozen trace.

and initialization order of these compiled phase bodies. The
registration snapshots alone cannot classify a body as removable.

and datagram bodies. The phase-zero =types= object publishes
the datagram class before the API. Lean checks that projecting
the nine positive-phase sites preserves the exact phase-zero
order and all 20 eager providers. Removing the branch wholesale
fails those obligations.

The frozen initializer classifier accepts all nine byte-identified
staging phase bodies. The selected compiled declarations keep

defined by the matching lowered phase body. The source, interface
and binary files supply 27 exact pins. The existing delayed phase

The staging patch combines the prior native consumer with the
existing prepared import entry. Macro metadata can hold native

consumer. The Gerbil MCP balance and syntax checks pass for the

**** Recover the Socket API type binding before testing deferred visits

On the pinned staging executable, the nine ordinary positive-phase
sites use the normal =core-import-module= resolver at phase one.
The =Interface= and =TypedDefinitions= mark origins are already
present in their canonical contexts at every site. The observation
preserves the earlier trace, registry order, 74 context witnesses
and both Reader checkpoints. Lean checks the nine actual rows.

The previous prepared Address interface causes =(udp-socket address)=
to fail during expansion. The original Socket API context resolves
=InetAddress= to the phase-zero binding owned by
=std/net/address/types=. The prepared context has no binding.
One named import restores the original binding and does not reopen
the broad Address interface. Native expansion then succeeds.
The complete startup prefix through the first receipt is identical
before and after that restoration. The revised input and Lean
binding witness retain the rejected case.

The original typed UDP call reaches Darwin =bind= and returns errno
22. An independent C control rejects the 108-byte address union
and accepts the 16-byte IPv4 record at the same loopback endpoint.
The socket control preserves the original call and its errno, then
uses correctly sized addresses for the fixture's socket setup.
The unprepared and restored-prepared arms both receive
=#u8(73 19 41)=, complete a connected echo and retain all 20
original method values through the later imports. The JSON
benchmark inputs and watchdog are unchanged.

The native stage1 build for the deferred candidate is separate
from these two preflight arms. Both prepared arms in its queued
semantics use the recovered type edge so their comparison isolates
the positive-phase visits.

**** Rule out duplicate strong bindings and plan setup as the sole fix

The direct native phase candidate retains =(import :std/io)= and
the scoped socket phase projection. It still fails the PR #1
timing gate at both boundaries. The selected frozen reference
remains at 462.59 ms for Reader import and 725.57 ms for the first
JSON receipt in its qualifying cohort.

Reverse the actual prepared clause order as the native replay does.
Use the 74 observed export tables, count by phase and local name,
and clear the comparison at a missing provider or a named clause.
Across 69 compiled forms this covers 283 of 308 whole imports.
It projects 41,316 export descriptors, including 19,111 strong
rows. Within the covered portions of individual import forms,
184 phase-zero strong rows repeat the same canonical binding.
One =std/io/bio/api= form accounts for 141 of them. This is a
snapshot-based estimate of same-form duplicates. It does not
count duplicates against prior forms, and it does not measure
all live binding-table operations. An optimization limited to
these duplicate rows reaches 0.45% of the projected descriptors.

A separate six-pair startup diagnostic uses the same regular
native executable, code and library inputs, the fixed receipt, and
the 180-second watchdog. All 12 processes exit normally. The
candidate consumes all 74 prepared interfaces before the Reader
import checkpoint. Pre-import preparation takes a wall median of
53.63 ms, with samples from 45.58 to 66.55 ms. Reading and decoding
the 1.3 MiB common plan accounts for a 46.87 ms wall median and
45.15 ms of process CPU in that stage. Making the plan lazy by
checkpoint would not defer any of its 74 interface selections.

In these instrumented samples Reader import still runs 148.39 ms
slower in the manual-first median after subtracting both setup
intervals. Lean also checks the stronger condition that removing
all candidate setup while retaining the manual setup leaves the
candidate 148.383 ms slower in the manual-first median. This
counterexample holds for the observed samples with unchanged
subsequent costs. It bounds the setup-only improvement in the
diagnostic and is separate from uninstrumented timing acceptance.

**** Profile the Socket API's second-pass contract expansion

On the same pinned staging inputs, a separately relinked expander
records nested module and declaration intervals. Each exclusive
interval subtracts timed nested work. Four pairs, two per order,
use the original Reader receipt and 180-second watchdog. All eight
processes return the original JSON and exit zero. The candidate still
consumes 74 prepared interfaces during Reader import. A 1,000-call
empty control records 3.89 ms of enclosed process CPU, or 3.89
microseconds per timed operation.

| Reader region | Candidate versus manual, candidate first | Candidate versus manual, manual first |
|---------------+------------------------------------------+---------------------------------------|
| Resolve imports and construct descriptors | 21.61 ms more CPU | 27.17 ms more CPU |
| Install import bindings and evaluate their phase dependencies | 72.75 ms more CPU | 83.70 ms more CPU |

The candidate repeatedly spends 332.51 to 346.25 ms of exclusive
process CPU expanding 64 pending Socket API expressions, with a
338.89 ms median across its four launches. These times include
their macro expansion. They do not include the later evaluation
of the resulting expressions. The 64 sites are literal calls to
=load-module= introduced by the compiled Address projection.

The stock expansion still wraps each literal in =%#let-values=,
checks =string?=, and either calls =__load-module= or raises the
annotated contract failure. The uninstrumented native candidate
publishes all 64 complete forms in the same literal order as the
prepared inputs. In its datum projection the forms share one
template, with only the literal path varying. Lean instantiates
that template with all 64 paths and obtains the observed forms in
order. Lean rejects replacing them with plain calls as an exact
code projection. The first native witness expected a top-level
=%#call= and found none. The corrected witness preserves the entire
expanded contract form.

This determines the next compiler target. Emit and reuse the
expanded contract structure for the admitted literal calls. Preserve
its contract branch, source metadata and separate hygienic local
bindings at each site. Validate transformer identity and the scope
of referenced bindings before reuse, and measure the same two
Reader endpoints through the normal path. The verified datum
projection does not prove those syntax-object obligations. The
profile and witness give no new timing qualification.

**** Relocate the qualified Reader plan to one SDK library root

The previously qualified plan contains 71 common and three
specialized inputs. Its serialized forms contain 75 unique
absolute paths, covering the 74 interfaces and the shared
prelude, and carry no live expander contexts. A build-side
transformation rebases the two old roots into one SDK library
root. Reversing it reproduces the canonical serialized bytes,
including sharing within the source metadata. The relocation
also preserves the hard-link identity of all 75 routes in the
six packed native groups.

A fresh native process resolves all 74 relocated entries from
the single library root with the same compiled Address scope.
It returns the original JSON, captures the earlier =TMPDIR=,
and exits normally under the 180-second watchdog. It measures
39.566 seconds to Reader import and 57.426 seconds to first
receipt in this standalone smoke run. This run supplies no
paired performance claim. It explicitly supplies the relocated
plan. Default discovery and the standard compiler emitter
are still open.

The native expander scope crosses two existing entry points.
=core-import-module= expands the selected interfaces, then
the separately invoked =__eval-import= eventually calls
=core-eval-module=. The JSON facade may subsequently reuse
the Reader context. Admission must cover the relevant
runtime activation as well as recursive expansion and retain
ordinary completion when a deferred Socket interface is
explicitly requested.

**** Preserve the nine deferred phases across both default-entry boundaries

Four native processes run against the frozen 62-replay SDK.
Each reads the same 74 prepared entries, captures the earlier
=TMPDIR=, returns =[false,null,[true]]=, and exits normally under
the 180-second watchdog. The recorded lists are sets of the
nine deferred Socket phase image names at three checkpoints.

| Placement of deferral | Reader import | First JSON receipt | Explicit Socket import |
|-----------------------+---------------+--------------------+------------------------|
| Existing explicit plan | 0 | 0 | 9 |
| Recursive expansion only | 9 | 9 | 9 |
| Expansion through Reader activation | 0 | 9 | 9 |
| Reader activation and separate JSON facade import | 0 | 0 | 9 |

Both split cases start with zero selected phase images after
expansion. Forcing the Reader module through the ordinary
runtime path completes the group if the expansion scope has
already ended. Keeping the group pending through Reader alone
moves that completion to the JSON facade import. Applying the
policy to the separate facade step preserves the cut through
the receipt. Every arm explicitly imports Socket afterwards
and sees the same nine image identities.

The separate activation arms do not reapply the prepared
interface table. The next loader change can associate the
phase policy with the admitted Reader context and apply it
through its runtime activation. Facade reuse must identify the
same admitted Reader and installation generation. An unrelated
explicit Socket import must use the existing phase-completion
path. The Lean check covers the measured boundary counts and
their two-stage dependency. These are functional controls for
default integration, with the plan still supplied explicitly.

At first receipt, 13 of the 42 candidate-only modules are

group to check next. Their transformer consumption, cached
origins, observable initialization, and ordered failure prefix
still require qualification. In particular, the manual variant's

cannot authorize removing a runtime effect.

Use the existing =plan_least= and =exact_manual_parity= lemmas
over expanded binding, transformer and effect edges. The latter
requires the hand-selected set to be admissible for the same
roots. Preserve ordered original effects through
=plan_preserves_effect_trace=, then account for physical image

frozen Reader schedule.

The additional Lean model proves that uniformly translating
candidate costs translates the twice-median by twice that cost.
It verifies the original four six-pair cohorts. On those

nine. Repeated import preserves the raw C-struct and Socket class
identities and sends and receives the original UDP bytes.
A positive-phase import expands the ordinary Address interface.
A mutable registration is rejected. The negative-phase edge
preserves the earlier initializer and the same cached exception.
A corrupt mandatory C-struct image fails with exit 70 before
Reader import or a receipt on both engines.

All three arms use the same protected, installed library root,
including the packed images. The frozen arm uses the original
executable and explicit recipes. The manual arm uses that
executable with the five PR #1 modules in the override slot.
The candidate uses normal =gxi= with the fixed SDK registration
and no explicit plan. The run retains all six arm orders twice,
the original JSON, early-capture assertions and 180-second watchdog.

Paired medians in the unchanged repeat.

|-------------+------------+-------------------+------------------|
| Frozen explicit 62 replays | Reader import | 330.49 ms faster (6/6 faster) | 150.23 ms faster (4/6 faster) |

The first cohort also finished all 36 processes. With default gxi first, its median was 310.43 ms slower at import and 431.44 ms slower at the first receipt versus PR #1. The longest process took 28.038 s. Retain that failed gate and every observation. The repeat used unchanged input hashes, orders and watchdog. Its 36 processes returned the original receipt and exited normally. The longest repeat process took 10.826 s. The default candidate qualifies at both checkpoints and in both orders against the frozen and manual comparators. The installed SDK and source patch are retained for this exact scope. Pooling the 72 runs by actual order preserves a faster median in all eight groups. Between 8 and 10 of the 12 pairs favor the default candidate in each group.

The repaired package/facade executable =5483f50f94750c7e8d303591bfe63817a12ece724d698792e602b2e9311c8a9a=
is the frozen comparator for this and subsequent Reader work. The

284-object engine remains a historical comparator. The common
protected SDK layout and the POO package objects are unchanged.

Three instrumented pairs associate the exact 42 additional
registrations with their actual loader calls. The elapsed union of
these calls is 107.01 to 152.70 ms. This
accounts for a substantial share of the earlier 187 to 198 ms
paired deficit. The call spans include loader work and do not show
that every included initialization is removable. They come from
separate traced processes and do not replace uninstrumented timings.

| Branch | Extra objects | Median covered loader time | Observed range |
|--------+---------------+----------------------------+----------------|
| Socket and its address/runtime dependencies | 18 | 51.42 ms | 46.04 to 71.98 ms |
| File and tempfile | 16 | 42.35 ms | 41.62 to 58.63 ms |
| Other I/O | 8 | 18.62 ms | 17.90 to 22.08 ms |

Socket is the largest measured branch. Its API initializer takes
143.61 to 228.44 ms inclusive of nested work in these traces.
The current lowered API has 20 eager procedure aliases, 21 method
installations and 11 interface prototype constructions. Lean
requires the datagram class publication and the five alias
providers ahead of the API image. The seven distinct images are
absent in the manual comparison. Dropping the entire branch or
its datagram provider fails this check. Tempfile captures TMPDIR
during startup. Address parsing and serde rows also contain
initializer calls or registry writes. The lowered inventory
retains every one of the 42 rows with its native owner.

The test-only elision of =datagram~0= leaves the JSON import
able to proceed. The Gambit loader reports three undefined
references in =api~0= and the original datagram procedure is
absent. The normal arm publishes that procedure. This is a
concrete counterexample to qualifying a cut solely by observing
the JSON receipt.

The isolated ordered replay attempted to reduce evaluator dispatch
while preserving all proven effects. Native checks show a fallback
to the ordinary evaluator. Two independent guards were corrected.
The original tag was syntax-wrapped and the final API load was
stored as a plain compiled call. The helper accepts all 149 forms
when given the 64 certified loads, yet the live stack still takes
the ordinary evaluator. The runtime route is therefore unqualified.
The first attempts and all native receipts are retained.

MCP's two missing-path cases, repeated import, UDP, class
identity, existing failure-order checks and the injected Socket
client prefix passed. The POO path retains 62 contract reuses
and the Socket phase boundary 0/9. The Reader still imports
=:std/io=. Independent load traces show 269
registrations for the fallback experiment, 269
for the frozen repaired engine and 227 for
manual. The candidate and frozen engine register the same set.
Their traces swap =std/error~0= and =std/list/list-builder~0=.
The earlier trace of the same frozen bytes matches the current
candidate order, so this difference cannot be attributed to the
experimental change. The Socket startup and injected failure
prefixes agree.

The uninstrumented timing cohort was not started because the live
optimization did not engage. The earlier 187 to 198 ms deficit
remains the last qualified paired comparison against the manual
arm. The repaired 269-object engine remains the fixed baseline.
Reducing registrations further requires separating the required
publication from bodies that can be deferred with binding identity
and initialization order preserved.

**** Trace Reader startup and omit the auxiliary I/O interface expansion

The 259-registration implementation is a retained semantic and timing
counterexample. It misses the 226-object ceiling and the two manual
first-receipt medians by 379.25 and 375.73 ms. Its artifact remains
immutable. Subsequent candidates use the repaired 269-registration
executable as the frozen comparison.

The immutable 261-registration executable is the negative timing
control. Keep the repaired 269-registration executable as the frozen
comparison. A candidate must register at most 226 objects on the
original first POO JSON receipt and meet the manual timing in both
launch orders.

Profile 12 processes on the timing cohort's common SDK. Split
top-level expansion from evaluation while leaving the ordinary import
and evaluation handlers selected. Attribute nested work to its deepest
event. The receipts are 261, 269 and 227 registrations, with all nine
Socket phase images still absent before the first receipt. The earlier
custom-handler probe disables the admission guard and executes the
284-object fallback, so its measurements are excluded. Retain the
failure caused by probing the manual engine for a private admission
symbol. The corrected diagnostic treats the symbol as optional.

| Measured work inside the instrumented process | 261 first | Manual first |
|-----------------------------------------------+-----------+--------------|
| Expansion excluding image loads and activation | 20.96 ms more | 344.78 ms less |
| Image loading | 72.74 ms more | 36.79 ms more |
| Module activation excluding image loads | 103.13 ms more | 57.26 ms more |
| Socket/Address image loading and activation | 92.54 ms more | 86.53 ms more |

There are two instrumented pairs per order. The positive image and
activation gaps are repeatable in direction. The expansion delta
changes direction, so this series does not attribute the full
previous 165 to 369 ms deficit to expansion. Socket/Address is the
largest stable named residual activation branch. Its private
declarations include the 62 load-contract reuses and its runtime
initialization publishes the datagram class, methods and serde
registrations. Earlier elision controls establish those obligations.

Lean checks both measured inventories against the full list of 227
manual registrations. All of them are in the 261 and 269 arms. If a
cut modifies only the 34 extras in the 261 inventory, its lower bound
is 227. More shared registrations must disappear than the number of
extra registrations retained. Count registrations at the same
checkpoint and require genuine selection by the existing named
loader.

The isolated candidate starts from the 263-object semantic reference.
For JSON Reader's private phase-zero facade, the plan retains ordered
runtime edges and the original phase-zero object for each of
=std/io/dummy=, =std/io/counter=, =std/io/delimited= and
=std/io/detachable=. Its compiler-emitted programs replace 161
declaration operations with eight classified operations. The retained
runtime edges use the original providers through the existing loader.
The same-layout 263 control and the candidate have the same image
set after the four delayed phases are excluded. The auxiliary runtime
owners have the same relative order. The first full trace differed in
the early =std/error= and list initialization order. Two further
same-layout executions of each arm produced identical complete
phase-zero sequences, including the candidate's original trace. The
earlier control permutation and each repeat are retained. The four
positive-phase images are omitted at the receipt without
splitting any implementation body. The protected layout carries the
existing 88-guard plan for the controls and an independent 96-guard
projection plan. Both retain the original Reader import =:std/io=.

The runtime-only contexts stay scoped during expansion. Explicit
dynamic import of =:std/io= resolves its full ordinary exports and
executes the four positive-phase visits. The original runtime method
and class identities stay stable and no phase-zero image initializes
a second time. Preloading Dummy selects the ordinary plan. The
Reader retains 62 contract reuses and Socket 0/9. UDP returns
=#u8(73 19 41)=. Early TMPDIR, macro hygiene, positive-phase ordinary
expansion and failure-prefix controls pass. The Lean event model keeps
all runtime events in order and checks the private lookup rule. The
native controls check the assumptions on the selected installation.

| Recorded from launch | Scoped I/O | Frozen 269 | PR #1 manual |
|----------------------+------------+------------+--------------|
| First-receipt registrations | 259 | 269 | 227 |
| First receipt, ms | 6961.13 | 6897.94 | 6450.85 |
| Exit, ms | 7248.54 | 7224.93 | 6686.80 |

| Checkpoint | Comparator | Candidate first | Comparator first |
|------------+------------+-----------------+------------------|
| First receipt | frozen | 23.89 ms slower (3/6 faster) | 58.45 ms slower (2/6 faster) |
| First receipt | manual | 379.25 ms slower (1/6 faster) | 375.73 ms slower (1/6 faster) |
| Exit | frozen | 8.92 ms slower (3/6 faster) | 51.65 ms slower (2/6 faster) |
| Exit | manual | 435.88 ms slower (1/6 faster) | 423.88 ms slower (1/6 faster) |

The unchanged fixture uses all six orders twice, a 150-second
watchdog and a 2 GiB heap on one SDK layout. All 36 processes return 73
and exit normally. The first-receipt comparison misses manual time parity. The
259-object candidate has 32 extra registrations and adds no removal

R4 remains the frozen JSON Reader acceptance baseline. The candidate

=.o1= and =.scm= choices. The first SDK root ends the shadow search. Missing search directories
are skipped before path normalization. The first draft repeated
the missing-directory counterexample, returning 73 through the
ordinary path. Its package run was stopped and its samples are kept

Unrelated test tools and the =gerbil-mcp/= package prefix can remain
on the path. An earlier same-name provider sends the import through

The guard checks installed =std/= and =gerbil/= providers as a
conservative cover for the sealed Reader's dependencies. It can
fall back for a shadow elsewhere in those two namespaces. The Lean

search snapshot. It also proves fallback with an earlier same-name
provider, distinguishes prefix-relative interfaces from raw native
images, and checks the frozen and ordinary schedules on their
respective branches. The native controls verify real lookup paths.

** Measurements and counterexamples

| Workload | Ordinary imports | Automatic candidate | Manual narrowing |
|----------+------------------+---------------------+------------------|
| LL1 native import to first receipt, ten paired trials | 530.84 ms | 244.39 ms | 143.63 ms |
| LL1 native entry with explicit expander import, ten paired trials | 715.07 ms | 531.16 ms | 304.75 ms |
| LL1 native standard-library implementation objects before the first receipt | 89 | 35 | 42 |

The first two rows are per-arm medians on the recorded local macOS workload.
Their trial sets and entry points differ. The standard-library object counts
are for the native entry without the extra expander import. The later explicit
expander candidate also loaded 35 such objects, down from 51 in the preceding
automatic candidate. Its paired timing interval versus that preceding candidate
crossed zero. Treat ordinary imports as the semantic comparison and the manual
variant as a performance comparison.

The native candidate deferred the =datagram= implementation until the original
socket suite was requested in the same process. UDP send and receive then
passed. The manual change also bypassed the =tempfile= initialization that
captures =TMPDIR=. Changing the environment after the initial import exposes
that semantic difference.

Expansion of the real LL1 interface originally loaded eight phase-zero bodies.
Admitting bodies with a matching load-time identity restored the plan after
expansion. The native run skipped =datagram=, produced the same parser receipt,
and preserved the earlier =TMPDIR= capture. A body loaded without a recorded
identity and an otherwise identical artifact selected through a different load
path both made planning fall back. The initialized graph and the evaluator's
original import order are separate inputs. The evaluator processes LL1's
=std/list/list= dependency later than the native wrapper does.

The earlier dynamic evaluator prototype produced the correct =ABR=
initialization trace and deferred an unused body. Its later dynamic import
failed with =Unbound variable=. An opaque interface also left a child deferred
during fallback. The real LL1 dynamic entry continued to load =datagram=
eagerly. These results remain counterexamples to full dynamic qualification.

The earlier compiler candidate recorded in [[https://github.com/tao3k/gerbil/issues/2][Gerbil issue 2]] reduced some
transformer loads while increasing the total native object count and startup
time for MCP and POO. Those regressions justify measuring physical images and
first-receipt latency together during consumer qualification. They belong to the
earlier revision and do not establish results for the current candidate.

The follow-up distinguishes interface readiness from native body readiness
and carries ordinary evaluation through the entire fallback traversal.
The first SDK relink reached the existing 150-second watchdog and exited 124.
The rerun with the same limit completed. The ordinary, automatic, and opaque
fixture arms all preserved the =ABR= trace and returned 17. The automatic arm
kept the unused body absent at import, then returned 999 on explicit demand.
The opaque arm preserved the interface effect and loaded that body eagerly.
A later qualified reference and a later explicit import with pruning disabled
also returned 999. Crossing from eval to the native loader preserved =ABR=.

The real dynamic LL1 entry now also defers =datagram=. Before the first
receipt, the ordinary and automatic runs loaded 89 and 41 standard-library
phase-zero implementation objects, respectively, and 235 and 217 distinct
Gerbil =.oN= objects in total. Both exited naturally with the same LL1 receipt and
preserved the original =TMPDIR= value. These are single traces of each arm.
They establish residency and observable behavior for that input.

The prior candidate missed a deferred value through raw =##global-var-ref=.
The ordinary arm returned 999 and the automatic arm exited 70. An intervening
candidate realized unrelated bindings because it treated the generated binding
check around a direct call as a first-class escape. Both failures are retained.

*** Direct evaluator reflection

The next candidate recognizes the emitted primitive call and its generated
binding check. It evaluates the arguments once and realizes the selected
binding before the primitive accesses the original cell. An escaping observer
or global table enumeration uses the ordinary loader. This preserves the
original primitive identity and the required initialization order.

All 12 reflection cases exited 0. Both arms kept the =ABR= effect order. The
automatic arm loaded only the selected body for direct reads and writes. A
repeated read and subsequent ordinary import returned the same procedure, and
a write remained visible after ordinary import. A missing name and lexical
shadowing loaded neither deferred body. Escaping the primitive or enumerating
the table loaded both bodies through the ordinary path. The cases cover the
evaluator entry point. Reflection in arbitrary native procedures still requires
the existing conservative boundary.

| Latest trace before the first LL1 receipt | Ordinary | Automatic |
|-------------------------------------------+----------+-----------|
| Dynamic eval, distinct Gerbil =.oN= objects | 235 | 217 |
| Dynamic eval, standard-library phase-zero bodies | 89 | 41 |
| Regular native entry, distinct Gerbil =.oN= objects | 243 | 128 |
| Regular native entry, standard-library phase-zero bodies | 89 | 35 |

All four trace runs returned the same receipt and exited 0. The automatic arms
left =datagram= deferred and preserved the original =TMPDIR= capture. These
single traces are residency evidence. They also retain Gambit warnings about
unresolved globals during loading for further qualification. Two experimental
SDK relinks hit the build watchdog. The standard development build completed
and the test runs retained their original 150-second watchdog and 2 GiB heap.

*** Paired dynamic LL1 measurements

The benchmark used ten balanced paired rounds with an unchanged parser receipt,
150-second watchdog, and 2 GiB heap. Every measured process exited 0. These
per-arm medians are from the evaluator entry without dyld tracing. The manual
comparison narrows the LL1 imports as recorded above.

| Stage | Ordinary | Automatic | Manual LL1 |
|-------+----------+-----------+------------|
| Import | 3098.13 ms | 3761.14 ms | 1541.87 ms |
| First call | 0.28 ms | 38.03 ms | 0.25 ms |
| Import to first receipt | 3098.52 ms | 3835.87 ms | 1542.13 ms |
| Full process wall time | 4867.06 ms | 5656.42 ms | 2878.90 ms |
| Observed receipt to process exit | 1166.38 ms | 1140.41 ms | 610.89 ms |
| Peak RSS | 263.31 MiB | 264.84 MiB | 186.97 MiB |

The automatic candidate was slower than the ordinary arm in eight of ten
import-to-receipt pairs. The median paired penalty was 531.56 ms, with a
23.29 to 1362.73 ms paired bootstrap interval. The automatic candidate was
slower than the manual arm in every pair. The identical ordinary relaunch
control crossed zero for import-to-receipt latency. The comparison with the
previous automatic SDK also crossed zero, so this run does not attribute the
regression to the reflection change. Receipt-to-exit uses the parent process
observations of the output and exit, including the timing wrapper.

The dyld traces recorded four phase-zero bodies first loaded between import
completion and call completion in each automatic entry. They were
=std/io/util=, =std/io/bio/api=, =std/io/interface/base=, and
=std/io/interface/bio=. The ordinary entries loaded all four during import.
This identifies work shifted onto the first call. Import and first-call costs
both need measurement when changing the placement of activation. A future
performance candidate must account for time spent proving the import plan,
expanding the interface, and realizing the required bodies before the first
receipt. This prototype fails the dynamic LL1 performance gate.

The latest SDK also passed the original LL1, socket, binary serialization, and
three crypto test files in both arms. Those six files exercise the focused
behavior, and the full library and downstream package runs remain pending.

** Quint contract and runtime replay

The [[file:../../models/gambit/runtime_import_pruning.qnt][Quint model]] runs the ordinary loader and the candidate side by side. A
cell keeps its defining module, phase and slot. The candidate computes
reachability from resolved references and initialization roots, including a
transitive initializer reference. The trace records effect order, value and
cell identity, physical image residency, and publication by one of two
workers. Separate branches exercise lookup without macro execution, phase
helpers, named eval and reflection, opaque fallback, artifact replacement,
and repeated requests. A waiter may wait for another owner. A same-owner
reentry must use a valid available publication or end with an explicit load
failure. The model represents the latter outcome. Its progress property
assumes fair execution of an enabled completion or failure action.

The [[file:../../evidence/scenarios/runtime-lifecycle/runtime-import-pruning.manifest.json][shared verifier registration]] binds the model and both revision identities.
Quint 0.33.0 passed 15 deterministic scenarios and 12 negative controls in the first verified model version.
TLC generated 11,948 states and exhausted 1,693 distinct reachable states with
zero left in the queue. Safety and fair claim settlement passed. The shared
verifier also accepted the four expected counterexamples for a missed dynamic
read, a self-wait, replayed initialization and reuse of a stale resident body.
The first verifier attempt recorded the violations and rejected the trace
transport because it cannot decode record-valued function keys. The revised
model keeps each full binding key in the cell and uses numeric slots for the
finite carrier.

Five generated model traces drove six reflection operations in 12 Gerbil runs
under the same 150-second timeout and 2 GiB heap. Terminal residency agreed
for read, write, absent name, lexical shadowing, first-class observer and table
enumeration. The fixture also checks initialization order and the original
cell's identity across repeated and explicit demand. This is a replay of the
selected observations. Full source refinement, including native concurrency,
transformer behavior and loader exceptions, remains open.

*** Search-path change counterexample

Changing the search path after the dynamic LL1 import, before the first call,
returned the receipt in the ordinary arm. Each automatic arm exited 70 with
=Deadlock detected= during a nested call to =__open-module=. Resident body
reuse has the same failure. A controlled replay used the existing opaque
loader drain before changing the path. Both automatic arms then returned the
receipt and preserved the already selected reader body. The model's
=pathBarrierTest= requires that prior logical imports be realized under their
prior path before publishing the replacement. The drain was an isolated control. It preceded the loader candidate below.

*** Load-path transition candidate

The loader candidate uses the original search path to fulfill already admitted
imports before publishing a new path. Both =add-load-path!= and
=set-load-path!= use that transition. A claimed split initializer must publish
or fail first, even when an opaque observer has permanently barred pruning.
The temporary path transition does not permanently bar subsequent imports.
A transition with a missing old artifact leaves the path unchanged and releases
its temporary guard. An attempt to change paths recursively during deferred
activation raises an explicit error. Semantics of such module initializers
and wider native interleavings require further qualification.

The previous SDK exited 70 in the four LL1 automatic path-change arms. The
candidate exited 0 in all six ordinary and automatic arms, including resident
reuse on and off. It fulfilled the prior =datagram= import before publishing
the replacement. Separate cases preserved =ABR= and the procedure identity,
accepted a retry after a temporarily missing old path, and serialized two
concurrent additions without losing either path. A later LL1 import deferred
=datagram= after a compatible path addition. A replacement directory that
actually supplied a conflicting image made the new import load eagerly.

A delayed split-initializer witness exposed an early publication in the older
SDK and again in the first candidate when opaque fallback overlapped the claim.
Both negative controls exited 70. The final candidate waited for the initializer
and selected its original artifact in both concurrent cases. The extended
Quint model passed 29 deterministic tests, including 13 negative controls.
The shared verifier exhausted 3,222 distinct states, proved safety and fair
claim settlement for the model, and retained five model-checker counterexamples.
It does not cover all native reentry or scheduler interleavings.

The same SDK passed all 12 model-driven reflection replays. The original LL1,
socket, binary serialization and three crypto test files passed in both loader
arms. Steady-state LL1 traces retained 89 to 41 standard-library runtime bodies
before the first receipt for eval, and 89 to 35 for the native entry. These
checks validate a correctness change. The import performance gate remains open.

*** Reuse and attribution

Profiling separated the dynamic import from its first call. The automatic
graph plan took 94.26 ms median across four instrumented runs, with 7.26 ms
exclusive to the planner. The four late loads spent 28.46 ms in native image
loading at the first call. The 35 automatic-only images in the earlier trace
are wrappers containing the compiler's split initializer selector.

With the same compiled artifacts and exact runtime, resident reuse avoided two
late =std/io/interface= image opens and changed the count before the first
receipt from 217 to 215. The ten-pair first-call median was 83.21 ms with reuse
off and 52.92 ms with reuse on. Its paired interval crossed zero. The reuse
arm's import-to-first-receipt median was 5.209 s, compared with 4.421 s for the
ordinary arm and 2.498 s for the isolated manual LL1 arm. The auto arm lost
all ten pairs against manual narrowing. These trials leave the performance
gate open. The manual variant also deferred the =TMPDIR= initializer and
observed the changed environment in a separate trace.

*** Runtime macro phase visit

The matched LL1 traces put 112 phase-one images and 35 split-initializer
wrappers in the automatic load graph. The manual entry loaded 71 phase-one
images and zero wrappers. Its runtime body count was close to the automatic
count. This makes phase work and initializer packaging separate sources of
the remaining gap.

The compiler already emits a proof for phase blocks consisting of transformer
procedures and their generated marks. The candidate lets such a block remain
pending when a runtime binding's =macro:= property only names a transformer.
Properties that must execute code during declaration take the ordinary phase
path. The normal call expander resolves the macro property when expanding a
call. Both arms preserve =(import :std/io)= and use byte-identical native
images. The matched control disables only the leaf phase certificate.

In the =datagram= case the phase image remains absent after the facade import,
the focused import and a value reference. The first actual macro expansion
loads it. A stale certificate at import uses eager loading. In real dynamic
LL1 the number of images before its first receipt moves from 217 to 216, with
only =std/io/socket/datagram~1= removed. The phase-zero body and =TMPDIR=
initialization observations are unchanged.

The first version failed when the search path changed before the macro was
used. The delayed visit now enters the existing ordered loader queue. It
captures the admitted image and the mark contexts. A path change opens that
image before publishing the replacement, and later expansion uses the
original transformer. The original failure returned 70 and the final replay
returned 0 without opening the shadow image. Replacing the file in place
after admission produces an explicit error and does not execute its bytes.
Preserving the old transformer under that external mutation remains open.

The extended Quint model passed 31 deterministic cases and exhausted 3,798
distinct states. The shared verifier checked six counterexamples. The real
checks passed for effectful transformer initialization, ordinary phase-one
procedures, reexports, initialization order, 12 reflection runs, six prior
load-path runs, and the six original LL1, socket, serialization and crypto
test files in both arms.

The following medians use ten paired rounds on the same SDK. The raw runs
preserve the 150-second timeout and 2 GiB heap. The observed receipt-to-exit
interval includes the parent process observation and timing wrapper.

| Metric | Ordinary | Eager leaf | Deferred leaf | Manual LL1 |
|--------+----------+------------+---------------+------------|
| Import | 1044.96 ms | 1105.05 ms | 1022.20 ms | 460.72 ms |
| First call | 0.19 ms | 18.39 ms | 18.62 ms | 0.17 ms |
| Import to first receipt | 1045.16 ms | 1134.08 ms | 1040.26 ms | 460.93 ms |
| Full process wall time | 1974.37 ms | 1782.21 ms | 1688.94 ms | 907.10 ms |
| Observed receipt to exit | 443.94 ms | 385.84 ms | 376.12 ms | 150.81 ms |
| Peak RSS | 263.31 MiB | 269.10 MiB | 268.27 MiB | 185.55 MiB |

The paired first-receipt effect for this one phase image crossed zero. The
paired median saving was negative 8.55 ms with a 95 percent bootstrap interval
of negative 170.25 to positive 267.75 ms. Peak RSS fell in all ten matched
pairs with a median saving of 0.81 MiB. The manual reference won all ten
first-receipt comparisons and loaded 141 images. Its separate trace again
observed the changed =TMPDIR=, consistent with omitting an earlier effect.
The candidate has a bounded residency benefit and leaves the performance
admission open.

*** Runtime initialization from an already loaded phase

The LL1 census pairs all 35 initialization wrappers with a phase-one image
already present before the first receipt. Their file sizes sum to 8,410,016
bytes. This sum describes the artifacts on disk. It does not measure RSS.

The compiler prototype attaches the existing split initializer as a thunk to
its module's phase-one image. Creating the thunk does not run the initializer.
The loader records the thunk with the loaded phase image and its body witness
in the existing module state. It chooses the thunk only at the regular
initialization point and keeps the existing load claim, ABI checks and
publication path. Compiler and loader switches are opt-in for this study.
The ordinary wrapper remains available for the native entry and for any
missing or mismatched witness.

Matched LL1 traces for =std/io/interface/base= move from 216 to 215 native
objects. Only =std/io/interface/base.o1= is absent. Its phase-one image and
later implementation remain present. Repeated import and native load preserve
the identity of =Reader::t=. Wrong body and wrong phase proofs both use the
ordinary wrapper. The native entry uses the wrapper without loading the phase.
A separate =tempfile= comparison with byte-identical images and its eager
phase already loaded also removes one wrapper. It retains the earlier
=TMPDIR= capture. With the phase deferred, toggling reuse leaves both arms on
the ordinary wrapper and opens no phase image.

The exploratory three-module compilation reached the original watchdog during
=std/serde/serialize=. A separate targeted =std/io/interface/base= compile
completed and reproduced the one-image change in both matched traces. The
original LL1, socket, serialization and crypto files passed in both arms.
Twelve generated reflection replays passed. The extended Quint model passed
34 deterministic cases and exhausted 5,996 distinct states, with seven model
checker counterexamples retained.

| Metric, ten matched rounds | Ordinary | Wrapper active | Phase reuse | Manual LL1 |
|----------------------------+----------+----------------+-------------+------------|
| Import | 1076.49 ms | 1019.78 ms | 1091.39 ms | 443.63 ms |
| First call | 0.20 ms | 17.79 ms | 18.29 ms | 0.17 ms |
| Import to first receipt | 1076.70 ms | 1037.74 ms | 1109.16 ms | 443.80 ms |
| Full process wall time | 1769.13 ms | 1744.58 ms | 1791.78 ms | 868.43 ms |
| Observed receipt to exit | 447.59 ms | 372.54 ms | 366.10 ms | 149.21 ms |
| Peak RSS | 262.47 MiB | 268.66 MiB | 263.70 MiB | 185.65 MiB |

The paired peak RSS saving was 4.94 MiB and all ten matched rounds favored
reuse on that metric. First-receipt latency had a paired median saving of
negative 30.77 ms with a 95 percent bootstrap interval of negative 146.75 to
positive 23.63 ms. The unchanged-wrapper relaunch also crossed zero. The
manual reference won every first-receipt comparison. The prototype reopens
interfaces on phase-one loads even when they carry no initializer proof.
Reusing the expander's existing parsed interface information would remove
that duplicated work before judging whether the technique improves startup.

*** Reuse the parsed phase initializer declaration

The loader used to reopen an adjacent =.ssi= file when opening each phase-one
object with phase initializer reuse enabled. The expander already reads that
interface. It now supplies the declaration from the parsed forms and retains
it with an admitted deferred visit. The loader checks the section name, image
location, and image fingerprint. The existing body fingerprint and
post-load phase verification still guard initializer execution. Native entry
without a parsed declaration keeps the ordinary wrapper. No new native
objects are emitted by this change.

The retained automatic LL1 trace contains 111 distinct phase-one images.
Exactly one adjacent interface declares a phase initializer carrier. The
inventory measures image residency. The source change removes the loader's
file-open path for this lookup. Of the 44 phase-one image identities found
only in the automatic arm of the earlier manual comparison, zero had a
deferred-transformer declaration in the selected build. Determining which
can stay deferred requires recompiling the relevant Scope with the proof
enabled and checking the resulting phase references.

The exact Gerbil and Gambit baselines were =dbdf7632276b678e34a4048e9f76554355532d62=
and =b3ff304f1bf49e58ead5f848dc81b3e0d4c75228=. The first two BACH
attempts crashed in the older bootstrap interpreter when it loaded the new
expander. Guarding the opt-in parser behind the existing runtime pruning
parameter let that interpreter take the ordinary path. The standard
=./build.sh stage1-bach= succeeded on the corrected source. The relink
reported two additional optional loader symbol warnings during bootstrap.

| Matched LL1 control | Outcome |
|---------------------+---------|
| Before and after, same carrier overlay | Identical 215 physical objects, parser receipt, and initial =TMPDIR= capture |
| Wrapper path with phase reuse off | 216 objects. Only =std/io/interface/base.o1= is added. |
| Wrong phase or body fingerprint | Same normalized images as the wrapper control |
| Identical phase bytes at another load-path location | Same prior and candidate fallback, with 221 images and the ordinary wrapper |
| Tempfile phase left deferred | Ordinary wrapper and no phase-one tempfile image before the receipt |
| Admitted tempfile phase activated later | Both SDKs use 215 objects. Disabling reuse adds only =std/io/tempfile.o1=. |
| Native entry before the phase is open | Ordinary wrapper without opening the phase |

Both repeated-import checks retained the same =Reader::t= binding. Twelve
native reflection runs matched the Quint trace projections. The unchanged
LL1, socket, binary serialization and crypto test files passed in both
arms. The probes and timing runner remain in the isolated investigation.

The ten paired rounds used the same carrier overlay, input, 150-second timeout,
2 GiB heap and parser receipt. The same-candidate repeat kept its executable
and configuration.

| Ten matched rounds, per-arm median | Ordinary | Previous reuse | Parsed declaration | Manual |
|------------------------------------+----------+----------------+--------------------+--------|
| Import | 4906.72 ms | 5186.96 ms | 5645.82 ms | 2371.05 ms |
| First call | 0.50 ms | 63.26 ms | 164.34 ms | 0.25 ms |
| Import to first receipt | 4908.04 ms | 5313.95 ms | 5785.89 ms | 2371.40 ms |
| Full process wall time | 7962.48 ms | 8390.00 ms | 8774.37 ms | 3953.78 ms |
| Observed receipt to exit | 2029.18 ms | 1543.13 ms | 1744.20 ms | 692.02 ms |
| Peak RSS | 263.30 MiB | 264.08 MiB | 269.76 MiB | 187.19 MiB |

The paired median saving before the first receipt was
-280.23 ms with a 95 percent interval of -981.43 to +1141.70 ms.
The candidate was faster in 4 of 10 pairs.
The same-candidate repeat interval was -929.66 to +1913.66 ms.
First-call latency alone had a paired median saving of
-59.75 ms with a 95 percent interval of
-139.98 to -11.90 ms, a regression in this substage.
The paired comparison against ordinary imports had an interval of
-3207.62 to +736.39 ms. The round set leaves a latency benefit unproven.
The full-command CPU time recorded by macOS time had a paired median saving
of +0.06 seconds with a 95 percent interval of
+0.00 to +0.09 seconds. Candidate latency against manual narrowing had a
paired median saving of -3348.07 ms. This evidence
qualifies the observed loader behavior for the LL1 Scope. Broader latency,
the full standard library, MCP and POO remain open.

*** Defer the basic socket phase in the same LL1 Scope

A targeted rebuild of =std/io/socket/basic= emitted the existing
deferred-transformer declaration. Masking only that declaration gave a
control with the same three native image hashes. Both arms retained
the ordinary imports and the same parser receipt and initialization
observation. The eager arm opened 215 objects through the first receipt.
The deferred arm opened 214. The complete physical difference after
normalizing the test overlay path was =std/io/socket/basic~1.o1=.

Before first demand the image was absent in the deferred arm. The
explicit import and procedure value lookup still left it absent. Expanding
a call to the actual socket wait macro loaded the phase image once.
Unchanged TCP, UDP, connected UDP, and multicast test cases passed in
both groups. The corresponding test suite and named case receipts were
collected by raising only test output verbosity.

| Ten matched rounds, per-arm median | Eager | Deferred |
|------------------------------------+-------+----------|
| Import | 2341.82 ms | 3110.11 ms |
| First call | 27.39 ms | 34.43 ms |
| Import to first receipt | 2369.25 ms | 3226.01 ms |
| Process wall time | 3655.96 ms | 4791.88 ms |
| Observed receipt to exit | 844.28 ms | 941.17 ms |
| Peak RSS | 266.59 MiB | 265.68 MiB |

Peak RSS fell in all 10 pairs with a paired median saving of
0.88 MiB and a 95 percent interval of +0.84 to +0.98 MiB.
The paired median saving through the first receipt was
-592.60 ms with a 95 percent interval of -2545.27 to +161.43 ms.
The same-candidate repeat interval was -309.13 to +1674.10 ms.
This Scope confirms exact object removal and lower peak RSS. Startup
acceleration remains unproven.

*** Compose socket phase deferral with method initialization

The current LL1 candidate keeps =(import :std/io)= in the same source.
Code generation completed for all 43 phase-one image identities that
remained in the automatic-only inventory after basic socket qualification.
The existing transformer predicate accepted the generated source shape
of 21. Another 21 had other top-level forms in the phase body.
=std/format/api= had nine procedure definitions, including the phase-one
helpers =fold-format-string-for-stx= and =fold-format-ops-for-stx=.
The present admission rule leaves that interface eager. The source
classification is a build scout. A loader declaration still needs a
successful native build and an exact image fingerprint.

The native compiler emitted phase witnesses for =socket/api=, =client=,
=server=, =sockaddr=, =socket= and =stream=. Masking only the emitted
declarations provided matched binaries for each pair. The first combined
build returned the correct LL1 receipt and skipped six phase images, but
it loaded five phase-zero bodies that the earlier SDK had deferred.
Isolated substitutions attributed all five to =socket/api=. The
experiment had compiled an ordinary initializer declaration (version
2, loader version 4) in place of the existing method initializer
(version 3, loader version 5). Rebuilding =socket/api= with the existing
method-initializer option restored the original runtime admission.
The earlier and corrected-eager arms loaded the same 214 image identities
after normalizing overlay paths. The corrected deferred arm loaded 208.
Its only difference was the six phase-one images.

| LL1 loading path | Distinct images at first receipt | Additional phase-zero images |
|------------------+----------------------------------+------------------------------|
| Prior LL1 candidate, with basic socket already deferred | 214 | 0 |
| First six-module rebuild, eager phase controls | 219 | 5 |
| First six-module rebuild, active phase declarations | 213 | 5 |
| Corrected method initialization, eager phase controls | 214 | 0 |
| Corrected method initialization, active phase declarations | 208 | 0 |

Both corrected arms returned the same LL1 parser receipt and preserved the
original =TMPDIR= captured by =tempfile=. Explicit imports and value
lookup for the stream and client socket bindings preserved the deferred
state. Expanding each call opened its phase once. Repeated import
returned the same procedure. Corrupting only the client declaration
fingerprint restored that phase at startup and kept the other five
deferred. The first client probe supplied the wrong arity. Its syntax
failure was retained and the corrected probe used the existing five-argument
call. All six original LL1, socket, serialization, and crypto files passed
in both corrected arms. The named TCP, UDP, connected UDP, and multicast
cases passed.

The retained package profiles exercised POO slot access and a JSON
round trip, and MCP's dynamic JSON-RPC import and =initialize=. The same
six phase images were the entire difference in their first-receipt
inventories. Both preserved their receipts and exited normally. POO
used its existing timing driver for the paired runs and an independent
trace of that entry point checked its image set.

Each workload ran ten matched pairs plus a same-candidate repeat.
The process timeout remained 150 seconds and the heap limit 2 GiB.
The timed launch through macOS =time= emitted no dyld events. The image
counts below come from separate direct traces through the same workload
entry points. Receipt-to-exit is the parent observation interval.

| Paired workload, per-arm medians | Images | Import ms | First call ms | Import to receipt ms | Full wall ms | Receipt to exit ms | Peak RSS MiB |
|---------------------------------+--------+-----------+---------------+----------------------+--------------+--------------------+--------------|
| LL1 | 214 → 208 | 1023.64 → 1006.31 | 17.38 → 17.08 | 1040.96 → 1062.99 | 1697.53 → 1650.97 | 352.90 → 345.84 | 264.62 → 262.75 |
| POO | 280 → 274 | 1639.74 → 1594.60 | 0.24 → 0.26 | 1640.00 → 1594.83 | 2441.10 → 2392.84 | 653.47 → 614.39 | 304.77 → 302.91 |
| MCP | 255 → 249 | 1417.77 → 1466.39 | 0.55 → 0.52 | 1418.29 → 1466.90 | 2262.36 → 2250.75 | 687.09 → 667.03 | 285.48 → 282.99 |

| Paired savings | Receipt ms, median [95 percent interval] | CPU ms, median [95 percent interval] | Peak RSS MiB, median [95 percent interval] |
|----------------+------------------------------------------+--------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------|
| LL1 | +52.85 [-161.14 to +112.05] | +45.00 [+20.00 to +70.00] | +1.88 [+1.83 to +1.91] |
| POO | +10.57 [-134.48 to +193.24] | +65.00 [+50.00 to +90.00] | +1.88 [+1.76 to +1.95] |
| MCP | -29.89 [-86.55 to +284.18] | +45.00 [-10.00 to +110.00] | +2.48 [+2.41 to +2.59] |

| Same-candidate repeat | Receipt interval ms | CPU interval ms | Peak RSS interval MiB |
|-----------------------+---------------------+-----------------+-----------------------|
| LL1 | -85.63 to +135.38 | -30.00 to +20.00 | -0.05 to +0.05 |
| POO | -272.53 to +150.38 | -20.00 to +25.00 | -0.12 to +0.13 |
| MCP | -465.05 to +140.51 | -80.00 to +65.00 | -0.05 to +0.02 |

Peak RSS fell in every paired sample across all three workloads. LL1
and POO also showed a reduction in complete-process user plus system CPU
time. The MCP CPU interval and all three first-receipt latency intervals
cross zero. The POO and MCP observations cover these retained package
profiles. Full standard library and package suites, native MCP protocol
acceptance, and the comparison against manual narrowing and the
Homebrew-patched SDK remain open.

*** First-receipt footprint contract

The revised model checks the exact startup image set for a finite graph with
an unused branch, required initializer references, and a split initializer.
It derives the expected loading mode from the proof and phase evidence at
publication and freezes the observed set at that point. Subsequent qualified
demand can extend the current set while the startup observation stays fixed.
The phase-one visit, binding identity, ordered effects, and loader ownership
retain their semantic checks.

Three mutations preserve the original semantic checks and violate the startup
contract. They load the unused datagram branch, introduce an import proxy
image, or take the whole-graph fallback with an admissible plan. A separate
manual-style mutation omits required initialization and returns the same root
value. The effect comparison detects it. Quint 0.33.0 passed 39 deterministic cases.
TLC exhausted 6,050 distinct states and passed safety and fair claim
settlement. The verifier accepted all 10 expected counterexamples. It
rejected the first version of the proxy control because its initial state
already violated the new invariant. The corrected fixture starts with the
expected precise set and violates it only after loading the proxy.

The generated traces were projected onto native observations from the same
local SDK and the existing dynamic LL1 workload. The original LL1 source keeps
=:std/io=. The manual comparison uses the recorded LL1 import patch. All six
runs had the same parser receipt and exited 0.

| LL1 replay arm | Distinct physical =.oN= images through first receipt | =datagram= images at first receipt | =tempfile= retains initial =TMPDIR= |
|----------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------|
| Ordinary | 235 | Phase and body | Yes |
| Automatic | 215 | Absent | Yes |
| Manual narrowing | 141 | Absent | No |
| Automatic, wrapper control | 216 | Absent | Yes |
| Automatic, stale body witness | 216 | Absent | Yes |
| Automatic, subsequent named demand | 216 | Absent | Yes |

The same-overlay wrapper control opened exactly one additional image,
=std/io/interface/base.o1=. A stale body witness also selected the wrapper
and preserved the normalized image set of that control. The separate named
demand replay loaded the =datagram~0= body once after the first receipt and
returned an identical Scheme procedure on a repeated import. Macro replay
tracked the real =datagram~1= image through facade import, value lookup, and
call expansion. The image appeared at expansion with a valid deferred
certificate. It was already resident during import in the eager and stale
controls. Twelve reflection runs also matched their trace projections.

The earlier ten paired rounds on this exact SDK give first-receipt medians
of 1076.70 ms for ordinary imports, 1109.16 ms for automatic pruning with phase
reuse, and 443.80 ms for manual narrowing. Manual was faster in all ten
comparisons. The ordinary versus automatic paired interval crosses zero.
The replay inventories include 91 standard-library image identities exclusive
to the automatic arm and 17 exclusive to manual, for a net difference of 74.
Of the 91 automatic-only identities, 44 are phase-one images. This inventory
identifies the remaining work. Eligibility to defer an image still requires a
binding, phase, or initialization proof for its Scope.

The native checks cover the selected trace projections. Full implementation
refinement, parity with the manual latency, and MCP and POO qualification
remain open. Treat the ordinary arm as the semantic oracle, the exact startup
set as the footprint gate for proven edges, and the matched manual workload as
a latency reference. Require all three checks before extending this Scope.

** Acceptance matrix

| Gate | Required receipt | Current status |
|------+------------------+----------------|
| Lean qualification | Independent ordinary observations cover the requested actions and fields. The graph, effects, image inventory and exit also need their own witnesses. | The combined Lean check passes with 30 new replay guards. Compiler lowering and the complete native dependency graph remain open. |
| Binding and phase semantics | Reexports, hygienic macro helpers, name lookup, actual transformer evaluation, dynamic binding access, reflective access and repeated import preserve original results and binding identity. | Explicit and qualified late demand pass. The evaluator reflection matrix passes. Wider native, macro and reload coverage remains open. |
| Initialization and fallback | Effects run once in ordinary order. Late demand works across evaluator and native entry points. Loader state survives repeated import, artifact replacement and failure. | Both path APIs pass the isolated candidate checks. Old same-input deadlocks and the early publication under opaque overlap are retained. Wider native concurrency and reentrant path effects remain open. |
| Real scope and performance | Loading traces prove that the unused implementation stays absent up to the first receipt, then loads on genuine demand. Paired trials preserve inputs, budgets and outputs. | The finite startup oracle passes. Projected checks confirm the datagram delay and wrapper cut. The prior basic socket observation moves 215 to 214 images. Composing six further phase declarations with the existing method initializer moves 214 to 208, and removes the same six images in the POO and MCP initialization profiles. Peak RSS falls in all three paired workloads. First-receipt intervals still cross zero and the manual latency comparison remains open. |
| Standard library and consumers | Run the unchanged full standard-library suite, MCP package and native protocol paths, and POO import and JSON round trip. Retain failures and successful replay receipts. | Original LL1, socket, serde and crypto suites passed in both arms on the latest local SDK. Full standard library, MCP and POO are open. |
| Release comparison | Use exact Gerbil, Gambit, package, and SDK identities. Compare ordinary, automatic, manual and comparable Homebrew variants. Record import, first call, first receipt, process exit, image counts and peak RSS. | Focused macOS observations only. No release admission. |

When measuring performance, retain the monitored workload and distinguish total
process wall time from the interval between receipt and exit. Archive raw
timings, exit status, image identities and receipts together. Each production
change should report its individual mechanism and acceptance evidence. Keep
temporary diagnostics and fixtures in the isolated investigation directory.
